## Rabbit-Headed Deer
*The original foundling: the same exposure produces a gentle grazer or a cornered berserker, depending only on chance.*

The Rabbit-Headed Deer was the first creature anyone documented that the Catastrophe had changed, and it is the reason many villages stopped shooting everything strange on sight. An ordinary deer, caught at the edge of a young rift, came out with a long, soft rabbit's head, tall ears and a rack of antlers that regrows in a pale violet glow. They moved in herds before the change, and they still do. Within a year, hunters had stopped calling them monsters and started calling them neighbors.

**One herd, two outcomes.** A herd that grazes near a rift for a season is rarely harmed by it. The Mutated Rabbit-Headed Deer is not older or sicker than its kin: it is the animal that took a sudden, concentrated dose, a surge at close range or a spell cast too near. The same dose can leave one deer calm and the next one furious, and nobody has found a rule for which. A herd can hold both, and the gentle ones keep the aggressive ones at its edges.

**What they respond to.** They don't speak, and they don't think in sentences, but the gentle ones understand a handful of Sylvan words and the tone of a voice, which is enough to keep them near people who feed them. The mutated ones are driven by a hunger for borrowed magic: they follow spellcasters, glowing weapons and anything that hums.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Rabbit-Headed Deer | 1/2 | Gentle herd animal |
| Mutated Rabbit-Headed Deer | 1 | Aggressive loner, hunts magic |
| Herdfather Stag | 3 | Herd leader, protector of the young |

**Family rules.**
- **Thin-Skulled.** Every one of them is weak to Thunder damage and to being Stunned or Deafened.
- **Ever-Budding Antlers.** They heal when bloodied unless Radiant or Fire damage has stopped the regrowth.
- **The scream.** Each can scream when badly hurt, a sound uncomfortably like a person's, which frightens non-Monstrosities and rallies other deer.
- **Unique quirk.** Each has one trick to be discovered: the Herdfather can be lured away by a mimicked fawn's cry.

{{monster,frame
## Rabbit-Headed Deer
*Medium Monstrosity, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 13 (natural armor)
**HP**  :: 27 (5d8 + 5)
**Speed**  :: 50 ft.

**Initiative**  :: +3 (13)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|14|+2|+2|Dex|16|+3|+5|Con|12|+1|+1|
|Int|4|--3|--3|Wis|14|+2|+2|Cha|8|--1|--1|

**Skills**  :: Perception +4, Stealth +5
**Senses**  :: Passive Perception 14
**Languages**  :: understands Sylvan but can't speak
**CR**  :: 1/2 (XP 100; PB +2)

### Traits
***Herd-Wary.*** The deer has advantage on initiative rolls.

***Ever-Budding Antlers.*** If the deer starts its turn with fewer than half its Hit Points and hasn't been damaged by radiant or fire damage since its last turn, it regains 3 (1d6) Hit Points, and its antlers regrow in a burst of soft violet light.

***Startle-Step (1/Day).*** When the deer takes damage, it can teleport, along with anything it is wearing or carrying, up to 15 feet to an unoccupied space it can see. This doesn't provoke opportunity attacks.

***Thin-Skulled.*** The deer has disadvantage on saving throws against being stunned or deafened, and thunder damage against it deals one additional damage per die.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The deer makes two attacks: one with its Antlers and one with its Hooves. It can't make both attacks unless it has taken damage this encounter or an ally or fawn is within 30 feet of it and threatened — otherwise, it uses only its Hooves to shove a foe away rather than gore it.

***Antlers.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +4, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 6 (1d6 + 3) Piercing damage.

***Hooves.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +4, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 5 (1d4 + 3) Bludgeoning damage, and if the target is Medium or smaller it must succeed on a DC 12 Strength saving throw or be knocked prone.

### Reactions
***Death-Scream (Recharges after the deer is reduced to half its Hit Points or fewer for the first time).*** The deer lets out a piercing, almost human shriek. Each creature within 30 feet that can hear it and isn't a Monstrosity must succeed on a DC 12 Wisdom saving throw or have the Frightened condition until the end of the deer's next turn.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Violet Velvet* (10 gp): antler-velvet that glows faintly for a day and, brewed into a tea, lets a creature shrug off one bout of fear (Advantage on one save against Frightened within the hour). The meat is wholesome and, unusually for a Catastrophe beast, safe to eat. *Startle-Bone* (25 gp): a small rabbit-jaw bone that makes a good component for a *misty step* scroll. A hunter's kit may hold a *Potion of Healing*.

**Lore.** It was the first creature anyone found that looked wrong in a gentle way, and it is the reason many villages stopped shooting everything strange. People who have heard its death-scream say it sounds uncomfortably like a person.

**Ecology.** A deer-sized grazer with a long, soft rabbit's head, tall ears and branching antlers that regrow in a pale violet glow. It lives in small herds at the edges of old rift zones, eats bark, moss and mana-rich lichen, and bolts at any loud noise. Does and fawns stay close; stags place themselves between a threat and the herd.

## Mutated Rabbit-Headed Deer
*Medium Monstrosity, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 14 (natural armor)
**HP**  :: 45 (6d8 + 18)
**Speed**  :: 50 ft.

**Initiative**  :: +2 (12)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|17|+3|+3|Dex|15|+2|+2|Con|16|+3|+5|
|Int|3|--4|--4|Wis|11|+0|+0|Cha|6|--2|--2|

**Skills**  :: Perception +2
**Vulnerabilities**  :: Radiant
**Senses**  :: Passive Perception 12
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 1 (XP 200; PB +2)

### Traits
***Magic-Starved Frenzy.*** If the deer can see a creature that has visibly cast a spell, activated a magic item, or wielded a glowing or humming weapon since the end of the deer's last turn, the deer must spend its movement closing the distance to that creature and, if able, attack it in preference to any other target — the itch of borrowed magic overrides everything else it might want to do.

***Ever-Budding Antlers.*** If the deer starts its turn with fewer than half its Hit Points, it regains 5 (1d10) Hit Points, and its antlers regrow crooked and asymmetrical in a burst of sickly violet light.

***Radiant Overload.*** Whenever the deer takes Radiant damage, it must succeed on a DC 13 Constitution saving throw or have the Stunned condition until the end of its next turn, its overloaded body briefly unable to process the further influx of power.

***Thin-Skulled.*** The deer has disadvantage on saving throws against being stunned or deafened, and thunder damage against it deals one additional damage per die.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The deer makes two attacks, using Antlers and Hooves in any combination. If the deer has fewer than half its Hit Points remaining, it can use Frenzied Bite in place of either attack.

***Antlers.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 7 (1d8 + 3) Piercing damage plus 2 (1d4) Force damage as jagged motes of unspent magic tear free of the wound.

***Hooves.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 6 (1d6 + 3) Bludgeoning damage, and if the target is Medium or smaller it must succeed on a DC 13 Strength saving throw or be knocked prone.

***Frenzied Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 10 (2d6 + 3) Piercing damage. The deer can only make this attack while bloodied, tearing in with far more violence than its frame should allow.

}}

\column
**Loot.** *Crooked Antler* (30 gp): jagged and sickly violet, it hums near spellcasters and works as a lure for other magic-starved beasts. *Frenzy Gland* (40 gp): an alchemist's component for a draught that grants a short burst of aggression and ignores fatigue, at the cost of caution. The meat is bitter and unsafe to eat (DC 12 Constitution save or Poisoned for a day). A pouch it has torn from a victim may hold a *Spell Scroll* (cantrip) it clearly couldn't read.

**Lore.** Hunters say the same herd can hold a gentle doe and a snarling cousin, and nobody can tell which is which until a spell is cast. The rabbit-head is the same, but the eyes are not.

**Ecology.** The same animal as the gentle deer, but wiry and scarred, with antlers grown asymmetrical and a mouth full of teeth that don't belong on a grazer. It doesn't herd so much as lurk, drifts toward any source of magic, and is most common in zones with a recent surge. Gentle deer shun it.

**Initiative**  :: +2 (12)

|Int|4|--3|--3|Wis|14|+2|+2|Cha|10|+0|+0|

**Skills**  :: Perception +4
**Senses**  :: Passive Perception 14
**Languages**  :: Understands Sylvan but can't speak
**CR**  :: 3 (XP 700; PB +2)

### Traits
***Herd-Wary.*** The Herdfather and each Rabbit-Headed Deer within 60 feet of it have Advantage on Initiative rolls.

***Ever-Budding Antlers.*** If the Herdfather starts its turn with fewer than half its Hit Points and hasn't taken Radiant or Fire damage since its last turn, it regains 7 (2d6) Hit Points as its antlers regrow in a burst of violet light.

***Calf-Call.*** A creature within 60 feet that mimics a distressed fawn's cry as an Action, and succeeds on a DC 14 Charisma (Performance) or Wisdom (Survival) check, draws the Herdfather's full attention. On its next turn, the Herdfather must spend its movement approaching the sound, and it can't take the Attack action unless a creature is within its reach.

***Thin-Skulled.*** The deer has disadvantage on saving throws against being stunned or deafened, and thunder damage against it deals one additional damage per die.
### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Herdfather makes one Antlers attack and one Hooves attack.

***Antlers.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 9 (1d10 + 4) Piercing damage.

***Hooves.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 8 (1d8 + 4) Bludgeoning damage, and the target must succeed on a DC 14 Strength saving throw or have the Prone condition.

### Reactions
***Herd-Scream (Recharges after the Herdfather is first reduced to half its Hit Points or fewer).*** *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 13, each creature within 40 feet of the Herdfather that can hear it and isn't a Monstrosity. *Failure:* The target has the Frightened condition until the end of the Herdfather's next turn. Each Rabbit-Headed Deer within 120 feet that hears the scream has Advantage on its next attack roll.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Herdfather's Crown* (150 gp): a full rack of violet-veined antlers, the largest trophy in any hunter's lodge and a fine focus for any druidic ritual of protection. *Calf-Call Reed* (30 gp): a hollow bone whistle fashioned from its leg that makes the cry of a distressed fawn, a lure that works on every rabbit-headed deer for miles. A herd's winter yard may hold a *Potion of Healing* or a *Healer's Kit* left behind by the villagers who feed it.

**Lore.** Every herd has one, and nobody knows how it is chosen. Villages that feed their local herd in bad winters call it the Herdfather and tell their children never to look it in the eye while it is eating.

**Ecology.** A stag the size of a horse, with a deep chest, a broad rabbit's face worn grey around the muzzle, and a rack of antlers that glows violet at the tips. It leads a herd of a dozen or more, walks at the rear on migration, and puts itself between a threat and the herd every time. In a mutated herd, the Herdfather is the one animal that still seems to remember being gentle.

## The Welded
*Fusion abominations, from a handful of small animals caught together up through the largest confirmed mass-casualty horrors.*

When a surge strikes a crowd, most people die or flee. Some do neither. They are caught together, pressed into one another by the same wave of magic, and the Catastrophe finishes the joining. What stands up afterwards is not a group of people but a single body with too many limbs, too many faces and no clear idea of where one person ends. These are the Welded.

**Size is a death toll.** A Welded is made in an instant, and what stands up depends on who was standing close together. A barn full of rats and crows and one unlucky dog becomes a Tangle. A village square becomes a Welded or a Huge Welded. A massacre on a bridge, a ferry or a refugee column can become a Gargantuan or an Elder. A crowd caught in water becomes a Drowned Welded. After the first moment, they keep growing by absorbing whatever wanders close, which is why the biggest are almost always the oldest.

**The voices.** Nothing in a Welded decides anything; the body acts on appetite and on the last thing the people in it were afraid of. But fragments of speech surface in voices that aren't its own, a name or a phrase from one of the people inside, and these are the only sign that anyone is still in there. The older the Welded, the louder the chorus. In the Elder Welded, some fragment of the many minds refuses to yield.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| The Tangle | 2 | Small animals fused together |
| The Welded | 3 | Fused crowd, Large |
| Huge Welded | 5 | Fused crowd, Huge |
| Drowned Welded | 6 | Fused in water, hunts from below |
| Gargantuan Welded | 7 | Hundreds fused, a walking massacre |
| Elder Welded | 9 | The oldest and densest, with legendary actions |

**Family rules.**
- **Fused Mass.** They have Advantage on saving throws against being Grappled, Restrained, or knocked Prone.
- **Chaotic Regeneration.** They regain Hit Points every turn unless Radiant or Fire damage has stopped it, and the biggest regain the most.
- **Many Voices.** When hurt, they have a chance to speak in a stranger's voice, which can Frighten anyone who knew that person.
- **Unique quirk.** The Tangle is stunned by a sudden loud sound. The Drowned Welded withers if it is kept out of water.

## The Tangle
*Medium Monstrosity, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 12
**HP**  :: 52 (8d8 + 16)
**Speed**  :: 30 ft., climb 20 ft.

**Initiative**  :: +2 (12)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|14|+2|+2|Dex|14|+2|+2|Con|14|+2|+4|
|Int|1|--5|--5|Wis|10|+0|+0|Cha|2|--4|--4|

**Vulnerabilities**  :: Radiant
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 10
**CR**  :: 2 (XP 450; PB +2)

### Traits
***Many Small Bodies.*** Fused from rats, crows, and at least one unlucky dog, the Tangle has advantage on saving throws against being grappled, restrained, or knocked prone.

***Skittering Chorus.*** The Tangle constantly emits an overlapping chorus of barks, squeaks, and caws. Creatures within 30 feet that can hear it have disadvantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on hearing.

***Chaotic Regeneration.*** The Tangle regains 5 Hit Points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 Hit Point and hasn't taken Radiant or Fire damage since its last turn.

***Sound-Struck.*** If the Tangle hears a single loud, sudden, dominant sound (a horn blast, a struck bell, a thunderclap), it must succeed on a DC 12 Constitution saving throw or have disadvantage on attack rolls until the end of its next turn, its overlapping cries thrown into confusion.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Tangle makes one Bite, one Claw, and one Peck attack.

***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +4, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 5 (1d6 + 2) Piercing damage.

***Claw.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +4, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 4 (1d4 + 2) Slashing damage.

***Peck.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +4, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 4 (1d4 + 2) Piercing damage.

}}

\column
**Loot.** *Chorus Gland* (20 gp): a bladder that still squeaks and caws when squeezed; an alchemist can fashion it into a one-use noise-maker. *Fused Pelt* (15 gp): a mat of fur, feathers and hide too patchy for a furrier but fine for cheap bedding. A collar still buried in the mass sometimes holds a small key, a coin or a *Potion of Climbing*.

**Lore.** Survivors argue about how many animals are in it, but all agree one of them is a dog that still looks for its owner.

**Ecology.** A hunched, fur-and-feather mass the size of a pony, a heap of rats, crows and at least one hound welded into a single body, with too many eyes, a dozen legs and a bleating, barking chorus. It haunts barns, ruins and midden heaps near rifts and tends to be the first stage of larger Welded.

## The Welded
*Large Monstrosity, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 13 (natural armor)
**HP**  :: 82 (11d10 + 22)
**Speed**  :: 30 ft., Climb 20 ft.

**Initiative**  :: +0 (10)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|18|+4|+6|Dex|10|+0|+0|Con|15|+2|+4|
|Int|2|--4|--4|Wis|8|--1|--1|Cha|4|--3|--3|

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Vulnerabilities**  :: Radiant
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Tremorsense 20 ft., Passive Perception 9
**Languages**  :: understands fragments of 1–2 languages but can't speak coherently
**CR**  :: 3 (XP 700; PB +2)

### Traits
***Fused Mass.*** The Welded is made of several overlapping, half-merged bodies. It has advantage on saving throws against being grappled, restrained, or knocked prone.

***Chaotic Regeneration.*** The Welded regains 10 Hit Points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 Hit Point and hasn't taken Radiant or Fire damage since its last turn.

***Many Voices.*** Whenever the Welded takes damage, it has a 1-in-4 chance of speaking a short, broken phrase in a voice that isn't its own — a fragment from one of the people fused into it. If a creature that knew that person hears it, the GM may call for a DC 12 Wisdom saving throw; on a failure, the creature has the Frightened condition until the end of its next turn, more shaken by recognition than by threat.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Welded makes three Rend attacks.

***Rend.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 9 (1d10 + 4) Slashing damage.

***Grasping Limbs (Recharge 5–6).*** As a Bonus Action, the Welded makes a Grapple check (+6) against one creature within 10 feet of it, using one of its many extra limbs in addition to its normal actions this turn.

}}
\column
**Loot.** *Seamstone* (50 gp): a knot of hardened tissue from where bodies fused; it hums when held near a rift, and a clever artificer can use it as a component for any item meant to bind two things together. *Voice-Knot* (40 gp): a throat sac that still repeats a single word, prized by necromancers. Fused into its mass are often the belongings of the bodies it swallowed, such as coin purses, rings and a *Potion of Healing*.

**Lore.** Families sometimes recognise a voice in it, which is the worst part of finding one. Priests of the old faiths say it isn't a soul trapped inside but a hundred souls fighting to speak.

**Ecology.** A Large heap of several half-merged bodies, mostly humanoid and animal, with extra arms, shoulders and faces that pass through the mass. It moves slowly, climbs well and mostly stays near the place it was made, usually a rift mouth or an old killing ground. No two look alike, and the oldest are wider than doorways.

**Loot.** *Fused Marrow* (120 gp): dense, steaming bone from the core of the mass, used to bind golems and flesh constructs. *Bellows-Lung* (75 gp): a huge lung that still draws breath, wanted by divers and smugglers. Among the remains there is often a sack of coin and a *Spell Scroll* (2nd level) from a victim who tried to flee.

**Lore.** Mothers use it as a warning, because it grew from those who stayed together too long in a bad place. The old guard remembers one that came out of a shrine's cellar saying a prayer.

**Ecology.** A Huge, rounded pile of overlapping bodies, bigger than a cart, with dozens of limbs that never agree on direction. It stays near the place where it formed, grows slowly by absorbing what wanders too close, and is drawn to warmth and noise.

### Traits
***Amphibious.*** The Drowned Welded can breathe air and water.

***Fused Mass.*** The Drowned Welded has Advantage on saving throws against being Grappled, Restrained, or knocked Prone.

***Chaotic Regeneration.*** The Drowned Welded regains 10 Hit Points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 Hit Point and hasn't taken Radiant or Fire damage since its last turn.

***Many Voices.*** Whenever the Drowned Welded takes damage, it has a 1-in-4 chance of gurgling a short, broken phrase in a voice that isn't its own. If a creature that knew that person hears it, the GM may call for a DC 14 Wisdom saving throw; on a failure, the creature has the Frightened condition until the end of its next turn.

***Waterlogged.*** If the Drowned Welded spends 10 consecutive rounds out of water, its Chaotic Regeneration stops functioning, its Speed is halved, and it has Disadvantage on Strength checks and saving throws until it is fully submerged for 1 minute.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Drowned Welded makes three Rend attacks.

***Rend.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +7, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 11 (2d6 + 4) Slashing damage.

***Drag Under (Recharge 5–6).*** *Strength Saving Throw:* DC 15, one Large or smaller creature within 15 feet of the Drowned Welded. *Failure:* The target is pulled up to 20 feet toward the Drowned Welded and has the Grappled condition (escape DC 15). While Grappled this way and underwater, the target can't breathe.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Tide-Knot Sinew* (120 gp): waterproof cord that never rots, prized by sailors and divers for rope that holds in any current. *Brine Bladder* (80 gp): a swollen sac of cold, salty fluid that, drunk, grants a few minutes of water-breathing but leaves a lasting taste of the dead. Its lair is a wrecked hull or flooded cellar, where the dead have left bags of waterlogged coin, a *Potion of Water Breathing* or a *Ring of Swimming*.

**Lore.** Ferrymen along the flooded coastlands tell of the barge that was too full on the night of the Catastrophe, and that the passengers never got off. They say that on still nights you can hear them still arguing about who gets to leave.

**Ecology.** A Large, bloated mass of drowned bodies and sea-things fused together, glistening and green-grey, trailing weed and rope-like limbs. It lurks in flooded ruins, harbors and estuaries, is slow and heavy on land, and fast and silent in the water. Like all Welded, it is never quite the same animal twice.

{{monster,frame
## Gargantuan Welded
*Gargantuan Monstrosity, Unaligned*

**HP**  :: 230 (20d20 + 60)
**Speed**  :: 30 ft., climb 20 ft.

**Initiative**  :: --1 (9)

|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|23|+6|+9|Dex|8|--1|--1|Con|17|+3|+6|
|Int|2|--4|--4|Wis|8|--1|+2|Cha|4|--3|--3|

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Vulnerabilities**  :: Radiant
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Tremorsense 40 ft., Passive Perception 9
**CR**  :: 7 (XP 2,900; PB +3)

### Traits
***Fused Mass.*** Advantage on saving throws against being grappled, restrained, or knocked prone.

***Chaotic Regeneration.*** Regains 20 Hit Points at the start of its turn unless it took Radiant or Fire damage since its last turn.

***Many Voices.*** As the base Welded (Volume I), DC 14, but with a full chorus: 1-in-2 chance per damage instance rather than 1-in-4.

***Overwhelming Grasp.*** Can have up to three creatures Grappled at once without penalty.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Welded makes five Rend attacks, or four Rend attacks and uses Collapsing Lurch.

***Rend.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +9, reach 20 ft. *Hit:* 16 (2d8 + 6) Slashing damage.

***Collapsing Lurch.*** The Welded throws its full mass at the ground. Each creature within 15 feet of it must succeed on a DC 16 Dexterity saving throw or take 18 (4d8) Bludgeoning damage and have the Prone condition; on a success, a creature takes half damage and isn't knocked prone.

***Grasping Limbs (Recharge 5–6).*** Bonus Action; Grapple check +9 against one creature within 20 feet.

}}

**Loot.** *Chorus Heart* (300 gp): a heart larger than a barrel, still beating and still murmuring. It can power a ritual that speaks with several of the dead at once. *Slab of Seamflesh* (150 gp): an armor-grade hide that resists blade and rot. In the lower cavities the dead have left caches of coin, a *Potion of Greater Healing* and sometimes a *Bag of Holding* whose owner could not be saved.

**Lore.** This one is not a rumor. Refugees say they heard it before they saw it: a thousand voices speaking at once, none of them in the same language.

**Ecology.** A hill-sized mound of fused bodies, many more than a hundred, with limbs thick as trees. It crawls rather than walks, leaves a furrow of damp earth and bone behind it, and lairs where a massacre has soaked into the ground. Smaller Welded sometimes follow in its wake.

**Loot.** *Concord Core* (700 gp): the oldest, densest part of the mass, which hums with a faint harmony of many minds; a wizard can use it as the focus for a ritual of binding or *speak with dead*. *Elder Seam-Cord* (200 gp): a rope-thick sinew that is stronger than steel cable. Its lair contains a trove of items from its absorbed victims, including a *Ring of Protection* or an *Amulet of Health*.

**Lore.** The Elder is what people mean when they say the Catastrophe is still going on. It has been gathering for years, and some say its voices have begun to agree.

**Ecology.** A towering, ancient mass of bodies and growth, scarred, patched and strangely orderly, the only Welded that moves with something like purpose. It can climb nearly any surface, prefers ruins and cathedrals, and returns again and again to the place where it first formed.

\page
## The Bloom
*Botanical abomination born of concentrated-zone overgrowth that never stopped growing.*

In a concentrated-magic zone, green things grow faster than anything can eat them. In the first year, that was a blessing: orchards that fruited twice, meadows that stood waist-high by spring. By the third year, someone noticed that the hedge had moved. By the fifth, it had begun to hunt. The Bloom is what happens when plants take in more power than they can organize, and the result is a single organism the size of a house that walks, grabs and feeds.

**How a Bloom spreads.** A Bloom drops seed, and the seed sprouts into Bloomlings: knee-high, thorned and tethered to their parent. Most are burned by farmers or eaten by something else. Those that survive and stay close become Blooms in a few seasons. A Bloomfather is what a Bloom becomes when it has sat on a concentrated zone for years with nothing to stop it, with roots that reach for a hundred feet and a canopy that shades a valley.

**Living near one.** Foresters learn to read the signs: a patch of green that is greener than the rest, birds that have stopped nesting in a hedge, a sweet smell on windless days. Blooms hunt by vibration through the ground, so walking heavily draws them, and walking lightly does not. They are blind beyond their tremorsense and slow to turn, and nearly all of them burn.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Bloomling | 1 | Seedling, tethered to a parent |
| The Bloom | 4 | Mature walking thicket |
| Bloomfather | 13 | Ancient, with legendary actions |

**Family rules.**
- **Overgrown Regeneration.** They heal every turn unless Fire damage has touched them since their last turn.
- **Fire.** All are vulnerable to Fire and resistant to nonmagical weapons.
- **Pollen and roots.** Poison clouds and grasping vines make a Bloom hard to approach and harder to flee.
- **Unique quirk.** A Bloomling cannot live far from its parent, and a handful of salt does what fire does at a smaller scale.

***Overgrown Regeneration.*** The Bloomling regains 3 Hit Points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 Hit Point and hasn't taken Fire damage since its last turn.

***Tethered.*** If no Bloom or Bloomfather is within 120 feet of it, the Bloomling's Overgrown Regeneration doesn't function and it has Disadvantage on attack rolls. It was never meant to be alone.

***Salt-Sick.*** As an Action, a creature within 5 feet can scatter a handful of salt on the Bloomling, dealing 5 (2d4) Necrotic damage to it and giving it Disadvantage on attack rolls until the end of its next turn.

***Multiattack.*** The Bloomling makes two Thorn Whip attacks.

***Thorn Whip.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +3, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 4 (1d6 + 1) Piercing damage, and the target has the Grappled condition (escape DC 11).

***Root Snare (Recharge 5–6).*** *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 12, each creature in a 10-foot-radius Sphere centered on a point within 30 feet of the Bloomling. *Failure:* 5 (2d4) Piercing damage and the target has the Restrained condition (escape DC 12). *Success:* Half damage only.

### Reactions
***Pollen Puff (Recharge 5–6).*** When the Bloomling takes damage, each creature within 5 feet of it must succeed on a DC 12 Constitution saving throw or have the Poisoned condition until the end of its next turn.

\column

**Loot.** *Seedpod* (40 gp): a sticky, thumb-sized pod that sprouts a harmless but fast-growing vine when buried, used by farmers to bind soil and by smugglers to hide a cache. *Bloom-Thorn Cluster* (15 gp): hardened thorns that make serviceable arrowheads. A bloomling's root-ball often holds a few coins and bones from earlier meals.

**Lore.** Foresters say you can find a bloomling by looking for the one bush that is greener than the rest. Burn it early, they say, or it will root, and rooted Blooms do not leave.

**Ecology.** A knee-high, shrub-like creature with thorny limbs, a cluster of pale buds and a root-ball that it drags behind it like a hem. It grows from the seeds of a Bloom and stays within a hundred paces of its parent, feeding on whatever small animals wander near. In sunlight it opens; at night it curls shut and waits.

Buried among its roots there are often bones, rings and a *Potion of Healing*.

**Ecology.** A Huge mass of thorned vines, bark and giant blossoms that move slowly across open ground, with a dense core and pollen that hangs like smoke around it. It roots in concentrated-magic zones, eats whatever walks into its roots, and spreads by dropping seeds that sprout new ones.

**Loot.** *Heartwood Core* (1,000 gp): dense, warm wood that sings quietly and is suited to a staff, a wand or a druidic focus of high rarity. *Elder Pollen Cask* (400 gp): sealed pollen potent enough to drug a dragon. A hollow in its trunk holds a lifetime of swallowed treasure, possibly including a *Staff of the Woodlands* or a *Ring of Spell Storing* swallowed along with its owner.

**Lore.** Some druids insist Bloomfather is not malevolent, just very old and very hungry. Nobody who has met it agrees.

**Ecology.** A towering, hundred-foot mass of ancient, gnarled trunks, vines and flowers, with a canopy that shades the whole valley and roots reaching far beyond it. It anchors in the deepest concentrated zones, draws smaller Blooms around it, and wakes slowly. The land around it is lush, silent and empty of birds.

\page
## Wild-Magic Elementals
*Not mutations of an existing species: magic given a body directly, born purely of ambient wild-magic instability.*

Most of what the Catastrophe left behind used to be something else. These creatures never were. They are what happens when wild magic gets thick and stable enough in one place to take a shape. They have no parents, no young and no ancestors. The first people to see one thought they were dragons, and some still do. The drakes are only the most easily recognizable of them.

**Born, not changed.** A Wild-Magic Elemental does not grow old, breed or die of anything but violence. It forms when the local wild magic reaches a threshold, grows if the zone grows, and fades if the zone closes. The Surge Drake is common in any active zone. The Prism Stalker prefers zones with open, bright sky. The Rift Titan forms only where a major rift has stayed open for a long time, and is often found at the center of a region that has been unstable for years.

**Weather with teeth.** They are territorial, hungry and quick to anger, and they are not stupid: a Surge Drake hunts cleverly. But they have no language, and nothing resembling a person is left in them. They are best understood as what the zone looks like when it has teeth. A party that learns what a zone is doing, such as which way the surges move and when the weather turns, has already learned most of what it needs to know about these creatures.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Surge Drake | 5 | Winged predator, breath of raw force |
| Prism Stalker | 8 | Crystalline ambusher |
| Rift Titan | 12 | Apex storm-beast, legendary actions |

**Family rules.**
- **Wild Discharge.** When hurt, they may release a burst of unstable magic that affects the area around them (use the surge table).
- **Resistances.** They resist Lightning, Thunder and Force.
- **Volatile.** The flying ones struggle to stay aloft once badly wounded, and the Stalker can't function in darkness.
- **Unique quirk.** Each one is vulnerable to something its form implies: the Prism Stalker, being crystal, is vulnerable to Bludgeoning damage.

**Loot.** *Surge Scale* (150 gp): a crackling scale that keeps a small charge and gives off a single *detect magic* pulse when snapped. *Ember-Gland* (200 gp): a gland that stores unstable force energy, a volatile and valuable alchemical reagent. A drake's nest may contain a *Potion of Resistance* (lightning), a *Wand of Wonder* or odd bits of metal fused by lightning.

**Lore.** Scholars disagree about whether it is a true drake or a storm that learned to want things. It is always found where the Catastrophe left a scar.

**Ecology.** A heavy, wingspan-wide drake with a hide that shimmers and crackles, a lashing tail and a breath that comes out as raw force. It hunts across the sky above wild-magic zones, nests in rifts, and struggles to stay aloft as it grows weak.

\column

**Loot.** *Prism Shard* (250 gp): a palm-sized crystal that splits any light that touches it into color, a prized focus for illusionists and a component for any spell that bends or redirects. *Spectrum Marrow* (400 gp): glowing, many-colored marrow that can be rendered into dye fit for kings. Where it dies, unclaimed treasures often lie in its glittering wake, perhaps a *Wand of Wonder* or a *Rod of Absorption*.

**Lore.** Sailors call it the false dawn: a shimmering streak that crosses the hills at first light, leaving the grass burned in stripes of color. Few have survived to see the second morning.

**Ecology.** A long, low, faceted predator the size of a horse, with a hide of living crystal that throws light in every direction and a head like a cut gemstone. It hunts at dawn and dusk when the light is low and bright, lies up in caves of glass and mirrored ice, and goes dull and slow in darkness. Wild-magic zones are where it feels most at home.

***Claw (Costs 1 Action).*** The Titan makes one Claw attack.

***Wingbeat (Costs 1 Action).*** The Titan flies up to 20 feet without provoking Opportunity Attacks.

***Tail Sweep (Costs 2 Actions).*** Each creature within 15 feet of the Titan must succeed on a DC 17 Dexterity saving throw or take 13 (3d8) Bludgeoning damage and have the Prone condition.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Storm-Shell Plate* (600 gp): crackling armor plating that can be worked into a very rare shield or breastplate. *Titan's Tooth* (400 gp): a dagger-sized tooth ringing with stored lightning, used as a focus for storm magic. Its hoard may include a *Staff of Thunder and Lightning*, a *Cloak of Displacement* or a *Rod of Absorption*, all half-melted by its aura.

**Lore.** Legend says one flew out of the sky the day the source broke and never stopped. Sailors are certain the storm they saw on the horizon that night had wings.

**Ecology.** A mountain-sized, storm-colored beast with a long neck, vast wings and a body wrapped in shifting, crackling shell. It makes its lair in the heart of a major rift, rises with every storm, and is apex wherever it goes. Wild-magic bursts follow in its wake.

\page
## Husk-Touched
*People who took in more of a concentrated zone's surplus than a person is built to hold, and never stopped absorbing.*

In the first weeks after the Catastrophe, there were places where the air tasted of copper and the water glowed. People who lived near those places were the first to be called Husk-Touched. They did not fall ill. They did not die. They simply stopped being able to stop taking in what was around them, and within a season they were hollow: pale, thin, still moving, and drawn to anything that carried magic.

**The slow fill.** A person who spent one night in a concentrated zone usually recovers. A person who lived in one for a season may become a Husk-Touched. One who stayed for years becomes a Brimming Husk, or worse. What matters is how often and how intensely the person was exposed, not how long ago it began. Frequent, heavy doses fill a person faster than a decade of occasional ones.

**The pull.** A Husk does not decide to feed. Its body has become a hollow vessel with a pull, and the pull is what moves it. It finds spellcasters through walls, drifts toward magic items like a moth toward a lamp, and drains life from whatever it touches to keep the hollow from collapsing. Rivers frighten them for reasons nobody has explained, and a few steps of moving water will turn a whole pack.

**Degrees of presence.** Most have no thoughts that can be heard. A Brimming Husk will, once, speak a single coherent sentence, a name, a plea or a fragment of memory, before it falls back into silence. The Hollow Speaker can command lesser husks. The Spillway Husk stores what it takes until it breaks. The Hollow King holds a court of them and communicates only in impressions.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Husk-Touched | 1 | Hollow drifter, pulled toward magic |
| Brimming Husk | 3 | Overfull, unstable, flickers of speech |
| Hollow Speaker | 6 | Commands lesser husks |
| Spillway Husk | 8 | Stores surges, then bursts |
| The Hollow King | 10 | Court-holder, legendary actions |

**Family rules.**
- **Magic-Sense.** They can find any spellcaster or magic item through walls.
- **Hollow Hunger.** Their drain heals them.
- **Running Water Aversion.** None will willingly cross moving water; being forced across hurts them.
- **Unique quirk.** Radiant damage doesn't just hurt them: killed by it, a husk disintegrates without a trace.

### Traits
***Magic-Sense.*** The husk can pinpoint the location of any creature currently under a spell's effect, or carrying a magic item, within 60 feet of it, even through total cover. Its senses for anything else are dim and inward-turned: it has disadvantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on sight or hearing.

***Hollow Hunger.*** Whenever the husk deals damage with its Draining Grasp, it regains Hit Points equal to the damage dealt.

***Running Water Aversion.*** The husk won't willingly enter or cross a body of moving water at least 5 feet wide, and if forced across (by magic, a shove, or difficult terrain), it takes 3 (1d6) Force damage as the current briefly unbalances the storm inside it.

***Overloaded Husk.*** If the husk is reduced to 0 Hit Points by Radiant damage, it doesn't die normally — it disintegrates in a silent flash of white light, leaving no corpse and no loot behind.

***Multiattack.*** The husk makes two Draining Grasp attacks.

***Magnetic Hunger (Recharge 5–6).*** The husk's hollow center pulls at everything nearby. Each creature within 30 feet of it that can see it must succeed on a DC 11 Wisdom saving throw or be pulled up to 10 feet straight toward the husk, not provoking opportunity attacks.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Hollow Resin* (25 gp): a pale, brittle residue that has a faint pull toward magic; alchemists use it to make a trap that draws spellcasters. *Spent Spark* (50 gp): a dull, empty gem that once held power, a component for any ritual that drains. Killed by radiant light, it leaves nothing at all. Otherwise it may carry a *Spell Scroll* it cannot use or a *Potion of Healing*.

**Lore.** It is what happens to a person who stood in a concentrated zone for too long and kept taking more. People say it never stops wanting.

**Ecology.** A gaunt, pale figure, vaguely human, with skin gone brittle and translucent and a faint glow inside the chest. It wanders the edges of concentrated zones, follows anything that carries magic, and avoids running water. Husks gather near their own kind but don't cooperate.

**Ecology.** A husk gone too far: swollen at the throat and chest, with cracked, glowing seams in its skin and a perpetual drip of liquid light. It stays near the heart of a concentrated zone, flinches at its own surges, and sometimes mutters a name.

It carries what it took from its victims, perhaps a drained *Wand of Magic Missiles* and a *Potion of Resistance* (force).

**Lore.** Husks don't die of old age, but sometimes they crack. The ones that do are called spillways, and you can see the glow through their ribs. Scholars of the Catastrophe say a spillway is a husk that has taken in more than it can hold, and that it knows.

**Ecology.** A tall, gaunt husk, translucent in places, with cracked seams that leak a pale light and a dull, listening stillness between bursts. It stalks the edge of a concentrated zone, drawn to any caster, and tends to be followed by a few lesser husks. When it vents, the ground around it is scorched in a perfect circle.

Its throne room holds the spoils of a dozen casters, perhaps a *Staff of Power*, a *Ring of Spell Storing* or an *Amulet of Health*.

**Lore.** The king is whoever the husks decide is king, and the husks always find one. The last wore the robe of a regent.

**Ecology.** A tall, regal husk, still partly dressed in rotting finery, with a cold light behind the eyes and a hush around it. It holds court in a ruin at the center of a concentrated zone, attended by lesser husks, and speaks only through impressions.

\page
## Siphonborn
*People whose bodies learned to eat what the Catastrophe left behind, and who cannot stop.*

The Husk-Touched are hollow: the surplus poured in, and nothing in them could hold it. The Siphonborn are the other answer. Their bodies did not fill up and break; they adapted. A Siphonborn has learned, without anyone teaching it, to draw magic in and burn it, and it will do so for the rest of its life. It senses the power in a spell slot, a wand's charge or a glowing blade the way a starving person senses bread, and it goes for it.

**How they differ from Husk-Touched.** A husk drains life and is pulled toward magic. A Siphonborn consumes the magic itself: spell slots, item charges, ongoing spells. A husk is empty. A Siphonborn is always, dangerously, full.

**Who becomes one.** Repeated, moderate exposure does it, not one great flood. People who worked in the old academies and temples, lived at the edge of a concentrated zone, or carried charged items through a surge week after week are the ones who become Gleaners. The Glutton, Spellreaver and Archon are further along, and they get there by feeding: the more a Siphonborn eats, the more it changes, and the more it needs to eat.

**The Surfeit.** Every spell level and item charge a Siphonborn consumes adds to its Surfeit. A little makes it stronger and tougher. Too much and it is Overfed: stunned for a moment and a danger to everyone near it. Feeding one is therefore a gamble, and the clever party decides when to offer a meal.

**Who is still in there.** The Gleaner is a hungry animal in a person's shape. The Glutton repeats one word, usually “more.” The Spellreaver speaks in sentences that stop halfway, and sometimes seems to remember being a mage. The Siphon-Archon is aware, and polite, and asks to be fed.

| Creature | CR | Awareness | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|:---|
| Siphonborn Gleaner | 2 | None | Scavenger of residue and charges |
| Siphonborn Glutton | 5 | A single word | Bloated, slow, dangerous to feed |
| Siphonborn Spellreaver | 8 | Flickers | Hunter of casters, borrows spells |
| Siphon-Archon | 12 | Aware | Holds court over a ruin, legendary actions |

**Family rules.**
- **Taste of Magic.** They can pinpoint any magic item or spell-affected creature within a set range, even through walls.
- **Siphon.** Their strikes can force a spellcaster to lose a spell slot, or an item to lose a charge, and they gain Surfeit for it.
- **Surfeit.** Too much and they are Overfed: stunned, and a danger to everyone around them.
- **Dead zones.** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, they starve, and the stronger ones take damage for it.
- **Unique quirk.** The Gleaner cannot resist a thrown magic item. The Archon will accept a meal as payment.

**Loot.** *Gleaner's Gland* (90 gp): a faintly glowing sac that holds the dregs of whatever it drained last, a one-use source of power for a spellcasting focus (a caster may regain a 1st-level spell slot by crushing it, once). *Hungry Glass* (40 gp): fine, brittle shards that sit where its eyes were; they flare near magic, and some scouts wear them as warning-charms. It carries what it took: stripped wands, spent scrolls and a *Potion of Healing*.

**Lore.** The first Gleaners were scavengers who learned to pick through the aftermath of a surge. The Catastrophe let them keep doing it. People say they used to be mages, and that is half true: most were servants, porters and students.

**Ecology.** A lean, stooped figure with a long reach, glittering eyes and a faint glow beneath the skin of its chest. It prowls the edges of battlefields, rift zones and ruined academies, feeding on the residue of spells and the charges in dropped items. It is cautious, quick and fascinated by anything that sparkles.

**Tactics.** It stalks casters and item-carriers, strikes from behind and falls back once it has fed. A party can lure it with a thrown wand or ring and catch it as it stoops. Too much magic at once makes it freeze up, so a high-level spell cast at it is more likely to slow it than to kill it.

It sits on a heap of consumed gear, possibly including a drained *Wand of Fireballs*, a *Potion of Greater Healing* or a stripped *Bag of Holding*.

**Lore.** Mages tell their apprentices about the Glutton. It is a man who ate a spell, then another, then another, and who has not stopped eating since. It will give you everything it has eaten, if you can find a way to make it stop.

**Ecology.** A hulking, swollen figure with a belly like a drum, pale thin arms and a small, hungry head set into broad shoulders. Its skin is stretched translucent in places, showing pale light beneath. It lumbers through old academy grounds and shrine complexes, drawn to anything enchanted. It moves slowly, and it never seems to be full.

**Tactics.** It walks straight at the nearest caster and tries to bite. Giving it a meal of spells makes it stronger for a while and then dangerously unstable; the Glutton's own bursts hurt it as much as they hurt you. Keep melee fighters in front and casters at range, and avoid feeding it high-level spells.

## Siphonborn Spellreaver
*Medium Monstrosity, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 16 (reaving mantle)
**HP**  :: 105 (14d8 + 42)

**Initiative**  :: +4 (14)

|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|12|+1|+1|Dex|18|+4|+7|Con|16|+3|+3|
|Int|14|+2|+2|Wis|14|+2|+5|Cha|16|+3|+3|

**Skills**  :: Arcana +5, Perception +5, Stealth +7
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 15
**Languages**  :: Common and one other, in sentences that stop halfway
**CR**  :: 8 (XP 3,900; PB +3)

### Traits
***Taste of Magic.*** The Spellreaver can pinpoint any magic item, or any creature under a spell's effect, within 120 feet of it, even through total cover.

***Surfeit.*** Each spell slot level or magic item charge the Spellreaver consumes, or each spell level it absorbs, adds 1 to its Surfeit. If its Surfeit reaches 10 or more at the start of its turn, it becomes Overfed: it has the Stunned condition until the end of that turn, each creature within 10 feet takes 11 (2d10) Force damage (DC 14 Dexterity saving throw for half), and its Surfeit resets to 0.

***Borrowed Working (3/Day).*** The Spellreaver can cast a spell of 3rd level or lower that it absorbed or drained within the last hour, requiring no components. Its spellcasting ability is Charisma (spell save DC 14, +6 to hit with spell attacks).

***Hunger Pangs.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the Spellreaver loses Taste of Magic, Absorb Spell and Borrowed Working, and takes 5 Necrotic damage at the start of each of its turns.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Spellreaver makes two Siphon Blade attacks.

***Siphon Blade.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +7, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 11 (2d6 + 4) Force damage. If the target has spell slots, it must succeed on a DC 14 Constitution saving throw or expend one spell slot of its choice; the Spellreaver regains 5 Hit Points per level of the slot and gains Surfeit equal to the level.

***Starved Rift (Recharge 5–6).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 14, each creature in a 30-foot Cone. *Failure:* 22 (5d8) Force damage, and any effect on the target that requires Concentration ends. *Success:* Half damage only.

### Reactions
***Absorb Spell (Recharge 5–6).*** When the Spellreaver is the target of a spell of 3rd level or lower, or is within its area, it makes a Charisma saving throw against DC 10 plus the spell's level. On a success, the spell has no effect on it, it gains Surfeit equal to the spell's level, and it gains Temporary Hit Points equal to 5 times the spell's level.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Reaver's Mantle* (600 gp): a ragged cloak woven of drained spell-threads, which absorbs the first 1st-level spell cast at its wearer each day, as the Absorb Spell reaction. *Spent Focus* (150 gp): a rod, wand or crystal emptied of every charge but still faintly warm, a collector's piece. It carries stolen spellbooks and a *Spell Scroll* (3rd level), perhaps a *Ring of Spell Storing* or a *Wand of Magic Missiles*.

**Lore.** The Reaver is a person who learned to ride the hunger. The ones who were mages when the source broke are the ones who understood what was happening, and decided to use it. They are sometimes still that, in flickers.

**Ecology.** A tall, rangy figure in a mantle of dull threads, with eyes like lit lamps and hands that are always slightly open. It prowls the cities and wizard-towers closest to concentrated zones, preys on casters and the occasional magical beast, and sometimes sits very still, as if remembering something. It may speak, once or twice, before the fight.

**Tactics.** It fights as a hunter. It opens with Absorb Spell on the first caster who acts, slips in to drain slots with Siphon Blade, and uses Borrowed Working to turn the party's own spells against them. A dead zone, an *antimagic field* or a party that plays without spells makes it clumsy and hungry.

**HP**  :: 157 (15d10 + 75)

**Initiative**  :: +2 (12)

**Skills**  :: Arcana +5, Perception +7
**Resistances**  :: Force; Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Immunities**  :: Charmed, Frightened
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 120 ft., Passive Perception 17
**Languages**  :: Common; speaks in a layered voice that asks, politely, to be fed

### Traits
***Taste of Magic.*** The Archon can pinpoint any magic item, or any creature under a spell's effect, within 120 feet of it, even through total cover.

***Feeding Field.*** Each time a creature within 30 feet of the Archon casts a spell of 1st level or higher, the Archon regains 5 Hit Points per spell level, and the caster takes 2 (1d4) Force damage per spell level.

***Concentrated Meal.*** While in a concentrated-magic zone, the Archon regains 10 Hit Points at the start of its turn.

***Starved by Silence.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the Archon loses Feeding Field, Concentrated Meal, and Taste of Magic, has Disadvantage on attack rolls, takes 10 Necrotic damage at the start of each of its turns, and can't regain Hit Points.

***A Seat at the Table.*** A creature within 30 feet that offers the Archon a meal (a magic item with charges, a drained item, or a spell cast willingly upon it) and succeeds on a DC 18 Charisma (Persuasion) check can make it stop attacking for 1 hour. The Archon accepts payment, then resumes if wronged.

***Unshaken Will (3/Day).*** If the Archon fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Archon makes two Slam attacks and one Siphon Maw attack.

***Slam.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +8, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 17 (3d8 + 4) Bludgeoning damage plus 5 (1d10) Force damage.

***Siphon Maw.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +8, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 17 (3d8 + 4) Force damage. If the target has spell slots, it must succeed on a DC 17 Constitution saving throw or expend its highest-level spell slot, and the Archon regains Hit Points equal to 10 times the slot's level.

***Starving Cry (Recharge 5–6).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 17, each creature in a 40-foot Emanation originating from the Archon. *Failure:* 45 (10d8) Force damage. *Success:* Half damage. Regardless of the result, all ongoing spell effects on the target end.

### Legendary Actions (3/Turn)
{{legend Legendary Action Uses: 3. Immediately after another creature's turn, the Siphon-Archon can expend a use to take one of the following actions. The siphon-archon regains expended uses at the start of each of its turns.}}

***Slam (Costs 1 Action).*** The Archon makes one Slam attack.

***Devour Spell (Costs 1 Action).*** One creature within 30 feet that is concentrating on a spell must succeed on a DC 17 Constitution saving throw or lose Concentration. The Archon regains 10 Hit Points.

***Starving Pulse (Costs 2 Actions).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 17, each creature in a 15-foot Emanation originating from the Archon. *Failure:* 18 (4d8) Force damage. *Success:* Half damage.

}}


**Loot.** *Archon's Maw-Crystal* (1,500 gp): a fist-sized, many-faceted crystal lodged in its throat, a legendary component for any ritual of consumption or warding. *Hunger-Heart* (700 gp): a large, glowing organ that, if preserved, restores a spent spell slot of the highest level to any caster who handles it properly, once. Its lair holds a century of drained treasures: a *Staff of Power*, a *Rod of Absorption*, a *Ring of Spell Storing* or an *Amulet of the Planes*.

**Lore.** The Archon is the thing that the Siphonborn become if they are left alone long enough. It is not cruel. It asks, politely, for what it needs, and takes it if refused. Scholars of the Catastrophe note that it is the only hungry creature in this book that says thank you.

**Ecology.** A towering, gaunt giant in a hide of dull, shimmering scales, with a mouth too wide to be called a mouth and a faint, steady glow behind its ribs. It presides over a ruined college, a drowned temple or a hollowed-out mountain that sits on a rich vein of magic, and tends a small court of lesser Siphonborn that serve as its eyes and mouths. It has a manner, and a voice, that people find almost kind.

**Tactics.** It begins by asking, and offers to trade. If refused, it fights with great weight and patience, and its legendary actions are designed to take away a caster's concentration. Feeding it too many spells makes it stronger. Depriving it, in a dead zone or an *antimagic field*, makes it frantic and slow. A party that can negotiate can often walk away.

\page
## Wellspring Kin
*The same concentrated-zone exposure that creates a Husk-Touched, gone right instead of wrong.*

For every Husk-Touched in the old zones, there is a story of someone who came out different: calm, warm, luminous and gentle. The Wellspring Kin are people who took in the same surplus as the husks and were not emptied by it. Instead, it settled. They glow softly, they heal, and they do not seem to want anything. Pilgrims have been seeking them out since the second year.

**Springs and their tenders.** Wellspring Kin appear beside springs, wells and pools of concentrated water, and each one stays at its water for good. A Kin tends a pool, a Warden of the Spring guards a whole zone, and a Wellspring Mother is the heart of a spring that has glowed for years. Nobody has been able to reproduce one, and the pattern is only that the water came first.

**What visitors learn.** They give what they can to anyone who asks: water, rest, a blessing, a bandage. They speak little, and some speak only in the same few blessings, as the Mother does. They will not fight to take, only to keep. Travelers who foul a spring, fell its trees or threaten the people around it find that the gentlest creature in the valley is also the most determined.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Wellspring Kin | 1 | Gentle tender of a spring |
| Warden of the Spring | 5 | Protector of a zone and its people |
| Wellspring Mother | 8 | Heart of a spring, a powerful healer |

**Family rules.**
- **Luminous Calm.** Resting near them grants protection against fear and poison.
- **Peaceable.** They never attack unless the spring, the zone or an ally is directly threatened.
- **Healing.** All can heal allies, and the stronger ones can do it more than once.
- **Unique quirk.** The Mother's power is tied to her spring: foul it, and she fails.

**Loot.** *Mother's Phial* (300 gp): water from the spring she tended, still glowing. Drunk, it restores as a *Potion of Greater Healing* and removes one level of Exhaustion. *Spring-Stone* (120 gp): a worn river-stone that stays warm to the touch, sought by pilgrims. The shrine around her holds offerings from a hundred years of thanks, possibly including a *Staff of Healing* or an *Amulet of Health*.

**Lore.** Some springs have a Mother, and some have none. The villages that do refuse to say which. They say she simply appeared the first night the water started to glow, and she has not moved since.

**Ecology.** A tall, broad figure in simple clothes, with luminous skin the color of pale gold, hair like threads of light and a staff worn smooth by hands. She sits by the spring and gives what she can to anyone who asks: water, rest, a blessing, a bandage. Her speech is a handful of phrases, repeated; her kindness has never once been the same twice.
\page
## Barrow-Kin
*People drained rather than overloaded: caught as a dead-magic zone was forming, and hollowed of whatever spark makes a person a person.*

If a concentrated zone gives some people too much, a dead-magic zone gives others nothing. When one forms, anyone standing in it feels something go out of the world, and some of them feel it go out of themselves. The people it leaves behind are the Barrow-Kin: grey, quiet and still, with no hunger, no anger and no spark. They are called Barrow-Kin because that is where they gather, in the old burial mounds and cellars at the heart of the zone.

**Where they gather.** A person caught at the edge of a dead zone while it forms, and left there, is the most likely to become one. Those who walk through briefly are usually fine. Those who stay are not. Barrow Wardens and Sunless are made by long, unbroken exposure, and they are the rarest, because a person who spends years in a dead zone often dies of something else first.

**Sun, silence and song.** A Barrow-Kin is a person with the lights off. They move toward warmth and sound, they touch, and they drain. Direct sunlight pins them down, which is why they hide in barrows and cellars by day, and why the Sunless, which walks in daylight, is so feared. Some keep a vigil at a grave, as the Barrow Mourner does. A few hum something old, and have been known to stop for it.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Barrow Mourner | 1 | Keeps a grave, silences spells |
| Barrow-Kin | 2 | The common type |
| Barrow Warden | 4 | Leader of a group |
| The Sunless | 7 | Walks in daylight, drains from afar |

**Family rules.**
- **Magic-Numb.** They resist damage from spells.
- **Sun-Bound.** Direct sunlight pins them down and stops them attacking.
- **Withering.** Their touch lowers Hit Point maximum until a long rest.
- **Unique quirk.** Fire is the great weakness of most, and song is the Mourner's.

**Loot.** *Mourning Cloth* (25 gp): a grey shroud, worn thin, that dulls the sound of footsteps by half, a quiet gift for a thief. *Grave-Salt* (15 gp): a small bag of salt it kept on the grave, a component for any ritual of rest or peace. It carries what the family left, such as a ring, a prayer-book or a small *Healer's Kit*.

**Lore.** In the first year, families found them kneeling at the graves of people they had lost in the Catastrophe, wearing the clothes they were wearing the night it happened. Most were driven off. A few were fed and left alone, and kept coming back.

**Ecology.** A thin, grey-skinned figure in the remains of funeral clothes, standing very still beside a headstone, cairn or sunken grave. It does not eat or sleep and responds to sound, especially speech and music. It is the gentlest Barrow-Kin, and its quiet, humming mourning is the thing visitors remember.

**Lore.** Barrow-Kin are what a dead-magic zone leaves of the people caught in it: no hunger, no anger, no spark. Locals leave them alone in daylight and avoid them at dusk.

**Ecology.** A gaunt, grey, human-shaped figure with slack features and pale, dry skin, dressed in the remains of what it wore when the zone formed. It shelters in barrows, cellars and caves, becomes helpless in sunlight, and drifts out at night toward warmth and sound.

**Loot.** *Warden's Shroud* (90 gp): a grey burial cloth that mutes spells cast near it (Advantage on the next save against a spell, once per day). *Barrow-Key* (60 gp): a cold iron key that opens a tomb, crypt or vault no longer on any map. It may carry a *Potion of Resistance* (necrotic), grave goods or a ring of office.

**Lore.** Every Barrow-Kin group has one that stands a little taller and a little straighter. The villagers say they still remember how to be in charge.

**Ecology.** A taller, broader Barrow-Kin, still wearing the badge or sash of its former station: guard, priest, steward. It leads a handful of lesser kin to the same crypt night after night and returns to it before dawn.

It carries relics of the people it hunted, perhaps a *Potion of Greater Healing* or a *Cloak of Protection*.

**Lore.** People who have seen one in daylight say it is the saddest thing they have ever seen, and the most dangerous. It walks in the open like a creature that lost the habit of fear.

**Ecology.** A tall, wasted figure, paler than its kin, with open eyes and a steady, tireless stride. It is the only one of its kind that can stand in the sun, so it hunts by day. It tends to lead from the front and attracts Barrow-Kin to its path.

## Void-Touched
*Rarer and more deliberate than Barrow-Kin: people who didn't just lose their spark to a dead zone, but kept enough of a mind to resent magic on purpose.*

Not everyone drained by a dead zone goes quiet. Some come out with a single idea, and the idea is that magic is the problem. The Void-Touched keep a mind, a thin and cold one, and use it. They hunt casters, strip items and sit in a silence they have made. They are fewer than the Barrow-Kin and far more dangerous.

**A creed of absence.** A Void-Touched is a person who was in a dead zone at its formation and came out with their will intact, and angry. A Hollow Vessel has lived in the dead zone for years and become part of it. A Void Abbot is the rarest, a Void-Touched that has gathered a congregation and a creed. Scholars of the Catastrophe have started to call it a faith, and nobody is certain they are wrong.

**Telling a hole from a husk.** A husk pulls magic toward itself. A Void-Touched pushes it away. Spells fail near them, items go dark, and the air feels thinned. They can sense magic as a shape, not as light, and they treat everything else as dim. Speech is rare and flat, and awareness of what they were is rarer still.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Void-Touched | 4 | Hunts casters, suppresses items |
| Hollow Vessel | 8 | A field of silence on legs |
| Void Abbot | 12 | Leads a congregation of the silent |

**Family rules.**
- **Resistant to spells.** All resist damage from spells.
- **Null fields.** Spellcasting near them is penalized, and the stronger ones can erase spells and item properties.
- **Void Step.** They can teleport short distances through the quiet.
- **Unique quirk.** The Abbot's field fails if someone sings.

**Ecology.** A pale, still humanoid, stranger than its Barrow-Kin kin: eyes dim, movement smooth and silent, with a faint cold that spreads in a ring around it. It keeps to the edges of dead zones and goes after anyone who uses magic.

The abbey it keeps holds the drained relics of a dozen casters, perhaps a *Ring of Spell Turning*, a drained *Staff of the Magi* or a *Rod of Absorption*.

**Lore.** The Void Abbot is the one who gave the Void-Touched a rule. Before it, there were individuals who resented magic. Now there is a creed, and a congregation, and a place to be silent in.

**Ecology.** A thin figure in a robe the color of ash, hood back, face smooth and calm, eyes like cold glass. It walks the corridors of a dead-magic ruin or abandoned monastery, followed by a congregation of Void-Touched, and it prays with an absence that people can feel in their teeth. It keeps no bells, because it cannot bear them.
\page
## Wildscarred
*People caught in a wild-magic zone's endless instability, their bodies and behavior never settling into one final shape.*

A wild-magic zone does not hold still. A person who stands in one long enough finds that their body has stopped holding still, too. Wildscarred are those people: a patch of fur on the shoulder, a flicker of flame in the palm, a mouth where a hand should be, all arriving and leaving without warning. They are not one creature but a thousand slightly different ones.

**No two alike.** A traveler who sleeps a night in a surge may wake with a changed eye and be fine. A person who lives in one comes out the other side as a Wildscarred. A Chaos-Wrought is a Wildscarred that has stayed too long, and a Herald of the Fray is what a Chaos-Wrought becomes when it begins to carry the zone with it. The Babbling Wildscarred is the extreme case, a creature whose throat has become a dozen mouths and which says everything at once.

**Reading the flesh.** A Wildscarred does what the zone is doing. It lashes out, it flinches, it laughs, it freezes. Its attacks carry a die of whatever damage the zone sends, and it often hurts itself as it hurts others. A party that watches the creature, not the numbers, can tell when its body is turning against it. Flickers of the person it was surface now and then: a word, a name, a phrase.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Wildscarred | 3 | Unstable humanoid, random damage |
| Babbling Wildscarred | 4 | Speaks in tongues, blurts effects |
| Chaos-Wrought | 6 | Large, with a fraying aura |
| Herald of the Fray | 10 | A walking surge, legendary actions |

**Family rules.**
- **Chaotic Flesh.** At the start of their turn, they might hinder or help themselves, at random.
- **Random damage.** Their strikes deal an extra die of a type rolled each hit.
- **Overstimulation.** If hurt by three or more different damage types in a single round, they can be Stunned.
- **Wild Discharge.** When hit, they may release a burst of force that harms the attacker and themselves.
- **Unique quirk.** A Babbling Wildscarred's blurted word is neutralized if someone repeats it.

***Chaotic Flesh.*** At the start of the Wildscarred's turn, roll a d6. On a 1, it has disadvantage on attack rolls until the end of its turn, its body briefly turning against itself. On a 6, it has advantage on its next attack roll instead, a lucky surge of coherence.

***Overstimulation.*** If the Wildscarred takes damage of three or more different types in a single round, it must succeed on a DC 13 Constitution saving throw at the end of that round or have the Stunned condition until the end of its next turn, its body unable to process the conflicting input.

***Flicker of Self (1/Day).*** If reduced to half its Hit Points or fewer, the Wildscarred has a 25% chance to blurt out one genuinely coherent word or short phrase — a name, a plea, a fragment — before its nature reasserts itself and it continues fighting exactly as before. ***Multiattack.*** The Wildscarred makes two Chaos Claw attacks.

***Chaos Claw.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +4, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 6 (1d8 + 2) Slashing damage plus 3 (1d4) damage of a type rolled fresh each hit (1 Acid, 2 Cold, 3 Fire, 4 Lightning, 5 Thunder, 6 Force).

It may carry a *Potion of Healing* or a *Wand of Wonder*, still half-charged.

**Lore.** Wildscarred are what a wild-magic zone does to a person without finishing. They look like a different thing every week, and sometimes mutter a name from their life.

**Ecology.** A humanoid whose body never holds one shape for long: patches of fur, scale, glass or flame come and go. It wanders wild-magic zones and cannot settle anywhere, acting on impulse.

**Ecology.** A humanoid with too many mouths, in the throat, the palm, the ribs and the back of the neck, each one in a different language and a different voice. It wanders wild-magic zones and wet marshland, drifts toward conversation, and leaves a trail of half-finished sentences hanging in the air.

\page
## Chaos-Infused Vampires
*Vampires already fed on a world with too little of what they needed. The Catastrophe flooded it.*

Vampires existed long before the Catastrophe, and the old ones had learned to live on very little: a little blood, a little magic, a little patience. When the source broke, the surplus poured into the world and into them. Blood that had been still for centuries began to boil. The result is not a stronger vampire so much as a restless one, a creature whose body is now partly weather.

**Boiling blood.** Time as a vampire matters less than how often, and how intensely, a vampire has fed or stood in a surge. An ancient vampire that hid underground for ten years may be barely changed. A freshly bitten villager who spent one night in a rift zone can wake as something far worse. A bite taken inside a wild-magic zone doesn't make a normal spawn: it makes a Flickerfang within the night. A Bloodstorm Lord is a vampire that survived a surge it should not have, and a Sovereign has done so many times.

**Courts and thralls.** Vampires have always kept households, and the infused ones still do. The Surgewing swarms scout, the Flickerfangs and Spawn do the killing, and a Seneschal manages them. The Lords and Sovereigns hold court in drowned manors, ruined keeps and storm-struck hilltops. The household's hierarchy is the one thing the Catastrophe did not break.

**Who is still in there.** Like the other changed folk of this setting, these creatures are not fully in command of themselves. The infusion has its own hungers, and it steers them. Most are driven by it. A few, rarely, are aware of what they were, and these are the most dangerous of all.

| Creature | CR | Awareness | Typical Form |
|:---|:-:|:---|:---|
| Flickerfang | 1 | None | Gaunt, sparking, hunched |
| Surgewing Bat Swarm | 2 | None | Cloud of red-sparked bats |
| Starved Penitent | 4 | Lucid but fraying | Pale, hollow-cheeked, restrained |
| Surgebound Spawn | 5 | Loops (a phrase, a name) | Distorted humanoid, blood-lit veins |
| Wildblood Stalker | 8 | Flickers | Humanoid or beast, shifting |
| Bloodbound Seneschal | 9 | Aware, but oath-bound | Immaculate, pale, polite |
| Bloodstorm Lord | 12 | Fragmentary | Tall, crowned in red lightning |
| Sovereign of the Red Surge | 15 | Aware | Humanoid core, storm-wreathed |

**Family rules.** All of these creatures share the following, restated in each stat block.
- **Dead-Magic Withering.** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, a chaos-infused vampire loses its Regeneration, Red Surge and similar abilities, and has Disadvantage on attack rolls.
- **Sunlight and running water.** Most are destroyed far faster by sunlight than ordinary vampires, and none can cross moving water willingly.
- **Unique quirk.** Each has one obscure weakness of its own, which is meant to be discovered rather than listed.

## Flickerfang
*Medium Undead, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 13
**HP**  :: 33 (6d8 + 6)
**Speed**  :: 40 ft.

**Initiative**  :: +3 (13)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|16|+3|+3|Dex|16|+3|+5|Con|12|+1|+1|
|Int|3|--4|--4|Wis|10|+0|+0|Cha|5|--3|--3|

**Skills**  :: Perception +2, Stealth +5
**Resistances**  :: Necrotic
**Vulnerabilities**  :: Radiant
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 12
**Languages**  :: Understands the language it spoke in life but can't speak
**CR**  :: 1 (XP 200; PB +2)

### Traits
***Compulsive Counter.*** As a Bonus Action, a creature within 10 feet of the Flickerfang can scatter a handful of small objects (grain, seeds, salt, coins) across a 5-foot area. The Flickerfang must succeed on a DC 12 Wisdom saving throw or spend its next turn counting, with the Incapacitated condition, until it takes damage.

***Sunlight Hypersensitivity.*** The Flickerfang takes 10 Radiant damage at the start of its turn in sunlight, and has Disadvantage on attack rolls and ability checks while there.

***Dead-Magic Withering.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the Flickerfang can't use Flicker Step and has Disadvantage on attack rolls.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Flickerfang makes one Claws attack and one Bite attack.

***Claws.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 6 (1d6 + 3) Slashing damage.

***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 6 (1d6 + 3) Piercing damage plus 3 (1d6) Necrotic damage. The Flickerfang regains Hit Points equal to the Necrotic damage dealt.

### Bonus Actions
***Flicker Step (Recharge 5–6).*** The Flickerfang teleports up to 20 feet to an unoccupied space it can see, leaving a puff of red-violet sparks at both ends.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Flicker Sinew* (25 gp): a thin cord that twitches when held. An artificer can use it as a component for a minor teleportation trinket. *Counting Bones* (10 gp): small knuckle-bones, strangely well-ordered, that make a fine set of gaming dice. Its clothes sometimes hold a locket, a few coins or a *Potion of Healing* from a former life.

**Lore.** Among the bitten, the Flickerfang is the first and shortest stage. Most last a few weeks before something larger takes them or the sun does. Healers call them flickers because the hunger makes them blink between places.

**Ecology.** A gaunt, hunched figure of any human-sized race, skin gone waxy and veins lit faintly red, with fangs too large for the mouth and eyes that never rest. It lives in the cellars and outbuildings of the place where it was bitten, avoids daylight, and is drawn to blood and to noise. It can seem almost sorry, for a moment, when it finds its own hands.

**Tactics.** It attacks from the dark, blinks past guards with Flicker Step, and drags its victim toward shadow. A handful of salt or grain thrown on the ground will often hold it for a full turn, which is the surest way to separate it from a group.

## Surgewing Bat Swarm
*Medium Swarm of Tiny Undead, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 13
**HP**  :: 36 (8d8)
**Speed**  :: 5 ft., Fly 40 ft.

**Initiative**  :: +3 (13)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|5|--3|--3|Dex|16|+3|+3|Con|10|+0|+0|
|Int|2|--4|--4|Wis|12|+1|+1|Cha|6|--2|--2|

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, Slashing, Necrotic
**Vulnerabilities**  :: Radiant
**Immunities**  :: Poison; Charmed, Frightened, Grappled, Paralyzed, Prone, Restrained, Stunned
**Senses**  :: Blindsight 60 ft., Passive Perception 11
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 2 (XP 450; PB +2)

### Traits
***Swarm.*** The swarm can occupy another creature's space and vice versa, and it can move through any opening large enough for a Tiny bat. It can't regain Hit Points except through Blood-Spark, and it can't gain Temporary Hit Points.

***Echolocation.*** The swarm can't use its Blindsight while it has the Deafened condition.

***Sonar-Struck.*** Whenever the swarm takes Thunder damage or hears a loud, sudden, dominant sound within 30 feet (a horn blast, a struck bell, a thunderclap), it must succeed on a DC 12 Constitution saving throw or have the Deafened condition and Disadvantage on attack rolls until the end of its next turn.

***Blood-Spark.*** Whenever the swarm hits with its Bites, it regains Hit Points equal to half the damage dealt.

***Dead-Magic Withering.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the swarm loses Blood-Spark and Wild Spark and has Disadvantage on attack rolls.

### Actions
***Bites.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 0 ft. *Hit:* 10 (3d6) Piercing damage, or 5 (2d4) Piercing damage if the swarm has half its Hit Points or fewer.

***Wild Spark (Recharge 5–6).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 10, each creature in the swarm's space. *Failure:* 7 (2d6) Lightning damage and the target has the Poisoned condition until the end of its next turn. *Success:* Half damage only.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Spark-Wing Membrane* (30 gp per handful): a translucent wing that tingles when held. A skilled tailor can sew it into a cloak lining that gives Advantage on the next saving throw against being Frightened, once per day. *Red-Dust Guano* (20 gp): a potent reagent in alchemy for restoring stale blood, volatile enough to be a poor choice for the careless. A nest in a belfry or cave may hold a *Potion of Healing* and tarnished coins knocked loose from a corpse.

**Lore.** Villagers call them the Redwing and keep their shutters tight at dusk. They say the bats were ordinary until the night the sky burned, and that one in ten has a bite that burns like a coal.

**Ecology.** A boiling cloud of fist-sized bats with veins that glow faintly red under dark, ragged wings, trailing tiny blue-red sparks. They roost in belfries, caves and ruins close to a vampire's lair, serve as its scouts, and scatter at dawn. Without a master nearby they still hunt, but poorly.

**Tactics.** The swarm engages first, flowing into gaps and doorways to harass and drain. It uses Wild Spark on groups that crowd together and flees in a spiral if driven by light or a loud noise. A single bell, horn or thunderous blast can wreck it, and a *dispel magic* attempt or a dead zone stops its blood-sparks.
