## Starved Penitent
*Medium Undead, Neutral*

**AC**  :: 14
**HP**  :: 67 (9d8 + 27)
**Speed**  :: 30 ft.

**Initiative**  :: +2 (12)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|12|+1|+1|Dex|14|+2|+2|Con|16|+3|+5|
|Int|12|+1|+1|Wis|16|+3|+5|Cha|14|+2|+2|

**Skills**  :: Insight +5, Persuasion +4, Religion +3
**Resistances**  :: Necrotic
**Vulnerabilities**  :: Radiant
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 13
**Languages**  :: Common plus one other, spoken carefully and slowly
**CR**  :: 4 (XP 1,100; PB +2)

### Traits
***Vow of Abstinence.*** The Penitent won't attack unless it or another creature is attacked, or unless its hunger takes over (see Leashed Thirst).

***Leashed Thirst.*** At the end of each of the Penitent's turns, if a Bloodied creature is within 5 feet of it, the Penitent must succeed on a DC 13 Wisdom saving throw or its hunger takes over. Until the end of its next turn, it must use its Bite on the nearest Bloodied creature.

***Regeneration.*** The Penitent regains 5 Hit Points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 Hit Point and isn't in sunlight or running water. If it takes Radiant damage, this trait doesn't function at the start of its next turn.

***Name Recalled.*** A creature within 30 feet that speaks the Penitent's name from life, and succeeds on a DC 13 Charisma (Persuasion) or Intelligence (History) check, makes the Penitent pause: it has the Incapacitated condition until the end of its next turn. Each creature can do this only once.

***Sunlight and Running Water.*** The Penitent takes 20 Radiant damage if it starts its turn in sunlight, and 10 Acid damage if it ends its turn in running water.

***Dead-Magic Withering.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the Penitent loses Regeneration and has Disadvantage on attack rolls, but its Leashed Thirst can't trigger.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Penitent makes two Sorrow Bolt attacks.

***Sorrow Bolt.*** *Ranged Attack Roll:* +5, range 60 ft. *Hit:* 13 (3d8) Necrotic damage.

***Bite (Only While Hunger Has Taken Over).*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +3, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 4 (1d6 + 1) Piercing damage plus 7 (2d6) Necrotic damage. The target's Hit Point maximum is reduced by an amount equal to the Necrotic damage taken, and the Penitent regains that many Hit Points. The reduction lasts until the target finishes a Long Rest.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Penitent's Beads* (50 gp): a string of worn wooden beads, one for each person it refused to feed on, which a cleric of a mercy-god may use as a holy symbol. *Hunger-Scrip* (20 gp): a prayer or confession on paper, often in cramped handwriting, wanted by those who study the infection. Its camp holds a worn *Healer's Kit*, a simple *Spell Scroll* (*calm emotions*) or a *Potion of Resistance* (necrotic).

**Lore.** The Penitent is the rarest outcome: a chaos-infused vampire that still remembers who it was, and still tries. It is also the most dangerous to hurt, because each drop of blood that spills is a promise it may break.

**Ecology.** A pale, thin figure in a patched robe or ruined finery, always standing very still, its veins faintly lit. It lives in abandoned chapels and cellars, keeps sealed vials of animal blood, and takes the long way around the living. It tends the graves of the people it has failed.

**Tactics.** It avoids combat, retreats and talks. Pushed, it lashes out with Sorrow Bolt from a distance and keeps the fight away from bleeding allies. Do not reduce an ally to half HP near it. Speaking its name stops it for a moment.

**Initiative**  :: +3 (13)

|Int|11|+0|+0|Wis|10|+0|+3|Cha|12|+1|+1|

**Skills**  :: Perception +3, Stealth +6
**Resistances**  :: Necrotic
**Vulnerabilities**  :: Radiant
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 13

**CR**  :: 5 (XP 1,800; PB +3)

### Traits
***Spider Climb.*** The Spawn can climb difficult surfaces, including upside down on ceilings, without needing to make an ability check.

***Regeneration.*** The Spawn regains 10 Hit Points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 Hit Point and isn't in sunlight or running water. If it takes Radiant damage, this trait doesn't function at the start of its next turn.

***Red Surge.*** At the start of each of the Spawn's turns, roll a d6. *1:* Sputter. Its Speed is halved and it has Disadvantage on attack rolls until the start of its next turn. *2–3:* No effect. *4:* Quickened. It can take the Dash or Disengage action as a Bonus Action this turn. *5:* Sparking. Its Bite deals an extra 3 (1d6) Lightning damage this turn. *6:* Overflow. Each creature within 10 feet of it, including the Spawn, must succeed on a DC 14 Constitution saving throw or take 7 (2d6) Force damage.

***Invitation-Bound.*** The Spawn can't enter a residence without an invitation from one of the occupants.

***Sunlight and Running Water.*** The Spawn takes 20 Radiant damage if it starts its turn in sunlight, and 20 Acid damage if it ends its turn in running water.

***Dead-Magic Withering.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the Spawn loses Regeneration and Red Surge and has Disadvantage on attack rolls.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Spawn makes two Claws attacks, or one Claws attack and one Bite attack.

***Claws.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 8 (1d8 + 3) Slashing damage.

***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 7 (1d8 + 3) Piercing damage plus 10 (3d6) Necrotic damage. The target's Hit Point maximum is reduced by an amount equal to the Necrotic damage taken, and the Spawn regains that many Hit Points. The reduction lasts until the target finishes a Long Rest.

***Crimson Arc (Recharge 5–6).*** *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 14, each creature in a 30-foot Cone. *Failure:* 14 (4d6) Lightning damage. *Success:* Half damage.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Red-Vein Filament* (80 gp): a thin glowing thread pulled from the Spawn's arms that pulses with the last of its blood, usable as a component in a ritual or, once, as the base of a *Potion of Healing*. *Spawn's Fang* (45 gp): a long fang that stays cold, wanted as a ritual dagger and, rumor says, a proof of kill by guild hunters. Its lair often holds tithe-offerings, a *Spell Scroll* (2nd level) and a *Cloak of Protection* left by a victim.

**Lore.** The Spawn is what a vampire's bite makes in a zone that is still raw. It repeats one word or phrase when it feeds, usually a name, and nobody has ever been sure whether it is begging or accusing.

**Ecology.** A distorted humanoid in torn clothes, veins lit bright red, joints a little too loose and eyes that sparkle with static. It lairs in ruins and crypts near the vampire that made it, comes out at night to hunt, and clings to walls and ceilings. In bad surge weather the changes show: a limb longer than the other, a second row of teeth.

**Tactics.** It drops from ceilings, bites the strongest-looking target, and leaps back out of reach. Red Surge makes its behavior unpredictable, so no two fights go the same way. Running water, sunlight or a dead zone each ruin it, and it will not cross a threshold without an invitation.

**Initiative**  :: +4 (14)

**Skills**  :: Perception +5, Stealth +10
**Resistances**  :: Necrotic; Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks

### Traits
***Shapechanger.*** If the Stalker isn't in sunlight or running water, it can take a Bonus Action to shape-shift into a Medium bat, a Medium wolf, or a cloud of red-lit mist, or back into its true form. Whenever it changes form, roll a d6. *1–2:* a minor wild-magic surge occurs within 10 feet of it (GM's choice or table). *3–6:* nothing extra. In bat form it has a Fly Speed of 60 feet. In wolf form its Speed is 50 feet. In mist form it has a Fly Speed of 30 feet (hover), can't speak or take actions other than to return to its true form, and can pass through gaps of 1 inch.

***Misty Escape.*** If the Stalker drops to 0 Hit Points outside sunlight or running water, it turns into mist instead of falling unconscious, and regains 1 Hit Point after 1 hour if it isn't destroyed.

***Regeneration.*** The Stalker regains 20 Hit Points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 Hit Point and isn't in sunlight or running water. If it takes Radiant damage, this trait doesn't function at the start of its next turn.

***Spider Climb.*** The Stalker can climb difficult surfaces, including upside down on ceilings, without needing to make an ability check.

***Sworn Table.*** The Stalker can't attack a creature that has shared a meal with it within the last hour, and can't enter a residence uninvited.

***Sunlight and Running Water.*** The Stalker takes 20 Radiant damage if it starts its turn in sunlight, and 20 Acid damage if it ends its turn in running water.

***Dead-Magic Withering.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the Stalker loses Shapechanger, Regeneration and Crimson Tempest, and has Disadvantage on attack rolls.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Stalker makes two Claws attacks and one Bite attack. It can replace one Claws attack with a use of Surge Step.

***Claws.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +7, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 13 (2d8 + 4) Slashing damage.

***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +7, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 8 (1d8 + 4) Piercing damage plus 14 (4d6) Necrotic damage. The target's Hit Point maximum is reduced by an amount equal to the Necrotic damage taken, and the Stalker regains that many Hit Points. The reduction lasts until the target finishes a Long Rest.

***Crimson Tempest (Recharge 5–6).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 14, each creature in a 20-foot Emanation originating from the Stalker. *Failure:* 22 (5d8) damage of a type rolled once for the whole burst (1 Acid, 2 Cold, 3 Fire, 4 Lightning, 5 Thunder, 6 Force). *Success:* Half damage.

### Bonus Actions
***Surge Step (Recharge 4–6).*** The Stalker teleports up to 30 feet to an unoccupied space it can see.

}}


**Loot.** *Wildblood Phial* (350 gp): a vial of its unstable blood, a component for a potion that grants a short-lived shapechange or a chance of a wild-magic surge on the drinker. *Stalker's Pelt* (200 gp): a patchwork hide that shifts color and texture, used for a cloak. It carries a hunter's belongings, such as a *Cloak of the Bat*, a *Potion of Greater Healing* or a *Spell Scroll* (3rd level).

**Lore.** In the dead of night a figure is on your roof, and it has the shape of a man, then a wolf, then a bat, then nothing at all. Those who have survived it say it spoke once, three words, and asked them to repeat it.

**Ecology.** A tall, lean figure, human or beast depending on the hour, with veins that run like lit fuse-wire across its skin and a face that doesn't quite settle. It hunts alone along roads and rooftops, returns before dawn to a lair in a mausoleum or storm drain, and has no family. A fixed bloodline's features show under the changes: a scar, a nose, a way of standing.

**Tactics.** It stalks and shapes, ambushes isolated targets and avoids groups. If the party holds firm it uses Crimson Tempest and Surge Step to re-position. Break its flow with a shared meal or a dead zone and it becomes a very different foe.

**Initiative**  :: +3 (13)

**Skills**  :: Insight +6, Perception +6, Persuasion +8, Stealth +7
**Resistances**  :: Necrotic; Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks

### Traits
***Spider Climb.*** The Seneschal can climb difficult surfaces, including upside down on ceilings, without needing to make an ability check.

***Regeneration.*** The Seneschal regains 15 Hit Points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 Hit Point and isn't in sunlight or running water. If it takes Radiant damage, this trait doesn't function at the start of its next turn.

***Oath-Bound.*** The Seneschal serves a master, a vampire of higher Challenge Rating. It obeys that master's commands and can't take any action that would directly harm it.

***Guest-Right.*** A creature that addresses the Seneschal in correct courtly form and succeeds on a DC 15 Charisma (Persuasion) or Intelligence (History) check can request that it withhold its attacks for 1 minute. The Seneschal honors the request unless the creature or its allies harm any vampire.

***Sunlight and Running Water.*** The Seneschal takes 20 Radiant damage if it starts its turn in sunlight, and 20 Acid damage if it ends its turn in running water.

***Dead-Magic Withering.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the Seneschal loses Regeneration, Mesmerizing Courtesy and Command the Thralls, and has Disadvantage on attack rolls.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Seneschal makes two Surge-Edged Rapier attacks and one Bite attack.

***Surge-Edged Rapier.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +7, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 7 (1d8 + 3) Piercing damage plus 7 (2d6) Lightning damage.

***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +7, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 7 (1d8 + 3) Piercing damage plus 14 (4d6) Necrotic damage. The target's Hit Point maximum is reduced by an amount equal to the Necrotic damage taken, and the Seneschal regains that many Hit Points. The reduction lasts until the target finishes a Long Rest.

***Mesmerizing Courtesy (Recharge 5–6).*** *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 16, one creature the Seneschal can see within 30 feet. *Failure:* The target has the Charmed condition for 1 minute. The effect ends early if the target takes damage or if the Seneschal harms its allies.

### Bonus Actions
***Command the Thralls.*** The Seneschal directs up to two Flickerfangs or Surgebound Spawn within 60 feet, causing them to move and attack on the Seneschal's initiative count instead of their own for this round.

### Reactions
***Parry.*** The Seneschal adds 3 to its AC against one melee attack that would hit it. To do so, it must see the attacker and be wielding its rapier.

}}


**Loot.** *Seneschal's Signet* (500 gp): a heavy ring bearing the seal of a vanished house, wanted by the families who lost their records. *Ledger of Debts* (300 gp): a thin black book recording the vampires' obligations, a source of blackmail or a hunter's map. Its quarters hold fine clothes, silver and a vintage collection of *Potions of Healing*, plus a *Cloak of Protection* or a *Ring of Evasion*.

**Lore.** The Seneschal is the one who answers the door. It was once a steward, a chamberlain or a lawyer, and it runs the household of the undead as it ran the household of the living, and with the same politeness. It is very polite, and it is not safe.

**Ecology.** A neat, pale figure in a dark coat, with exact posture, immaculate gloves and an air of regret. It is lucid but bound to a master it cannot refuse, and so it is both the most reasonable and the most dangerous of the vampires. It keeps the books, manages the thralls and receives the guests.

**Tactics.** It begins with courtesy, offering terms and a chance to leave. If refused, it fights with the rapier at close range, uses Mesmerizing Courtesy on a key target, and commands its thralls from behind. Address it correctly and make a polite request, and it will often honor it. Strike its master and it will not.

***Misty Escape.*** If the Lord drops to 0 Hit Points outside sunlight or running water, it turns into a red-lit cloud instead of falling unconscious, and regains 1 Hit Point after 1 hour if it isn't destroyed.

***Regeneration.*** The Lord regains 20 Hit Points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 Hit Point and isn't in sunlight or running water. If it takes Radiant damage, this trait doesn't function at the start of its next turn.

***Spider Climb.*** The Lord can climb difficult surfaces, including upside down on ceilings, without needing to make an ability check.

***Red Surge.*** At the start of each of the Lord's turns, roll a d8 and apply a minor wild-magic surge effect (GM's choice or a prepared table) in a 15-foot Emanation originating from the Lord; the Lord is unaffected by it.

***Unshaken Will (3/Day).*** If the Lord fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

***Coronet of Static.*** A creature that ends its turn within 10 feet of the Lord must succeed on a DC 17 Constitution saving throw or take 5 (1d10) Lightning damage and have Disadvantage on its next saving throw to maintain Concentration.

***Mirror of the Self.*** The Lord can't abide its own reflection. If it sees itself in a mirror, still water or polished metal, it must succeed on a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw or have the Frightened condition until the end of its next turn.

***Sunlight and Running Water.*** The Lord takes 20 Radiant damage if it starts its turn in sunlight, and 20 Acid damage if it ends its turn in running water.

***Dead-Magic Withering.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the Lord loses Regeneration, Red Surge, Coronet of Static and Bloodstorm, and has Disadvantage on attack rolls.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Lord makes two Claws attacks and one Bite attack. It can replace one Claws attack with a use of Surge Step.

***Claws.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +9, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 14 (2d8 + 5) Slashing damage.

***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +9, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 9 (1d8 + 5) Piercing damage plus 21 (6d6) Necrotic damage. The target's Hit Point maximum is reduced by an amount equal to the Necrotic damage taken, and the Lord regains that many Hit Points. The reduction lasts until the target finishes a Long Rest.

***Bloodstorm (Recharge 5–6).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 17, each creature in a 30-foot Emanation originating from the Lord. *Failure:* 45 (10d8) damage of a type rolled once for the whole burst (1 Acid, 2 Cold, 3 Fire, 4 Lightning, 5 Thunder, 6 Force), and the target suffers a wild-magic surge effect. *Success:* Half damage only.

### Bonus Actions
***Surge Step (Recharge 4–6).*** The Lord teleports up to 40 feet to an unoccupied space it can see.

### Legendary Actions (3/Turn)
{{legend Legendary Action Uses: 3. Immediately after another creature's turn, the Bloodstorm Lord can expend a use to take one of the following actions. The bloodstorm lord regains expended uses at the start of each of its turns.}}

***Claws (Costs 1 Action).*** The Lord makes one Claws attack.

***Surge Step (Costs 1 Action).*** The Lord teleports up to 20 feet to an unoccupied space it can see.

***Bloodstorm Pulse (Costs 2 Actions).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 17, each creature in a 15-foot Emanation originating from the Lord. *Failure:* 18 (4d8) Force damage. *Success:* Half damage.

}}


**Loot.** *Storm-Crown Shard* (900 gp): a splinter of the red lightning that circles the Lord's head, a focus for a major storm or blood ritual. *Bloodstorm Vial* (500 gp): sealed in glass, it releases a one-use 15-foot burst of random elemental damage when broken. In its lair are trophies from its victims, perhaps a *Ring of Resistance*, a *Cloak of Displacement*, a *Wand of Wonder* or a *Staff of Power*.

**Lore.** Few people alive have met one and it is not known how many exist. Scholars of the Catastrophe agree on one thing: a Bloodstorm Lord is what a vampire becomes when it survives a surge it should not have, and enjoys it.

**Ecology.** A tall, regal figure, once human or something near, with veins that glow like furnace-wire, fine clothing scorched by lightning, and a halo of faint red static. It holds court in a drowned manor, a ruined keep or a stormy hilltop, attended by spawn and bats, and it barely sleeps. Its form may be a different race than the person it once was.

**Tactics.** It begins at range, arriving in a flash of red light, and tries to control the field with Bloodstorm and Surge Step while its minions pin targets. Between turns it uses legendary actions to keep the party off balance. It hates mirrors, and a clever party may use one to turn it for a round.

***Name It Once Had.*** A creature within 30 feet that speaks the Sovereign's name from life, and presents an object from that life, can make a DC 18 Charisma (History or Persuasion) check. ***Sunlight and Running Water.*** The Sovereign takes 20 Radiant damage if it starts its turn in sunlight, and 20 Acid damage if it ends its turn in running water.

***Dead-Magic Withering.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the Sovereign loses Regeneration, Red Surge, Blood Echo, its Fly Speed and its Force and Lightning resistance, and has Disadvantage on attack rolls.

***Multiattack.*** The Sovereign makes three Claws attacks and one Bite attack. It can replace one Claws attack with a use of Surge Step.

***Claws.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +11, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 15 (2d8 + 6) Slashing damage.

***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +11, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 11 (1d10 + 6) Piercing damage plus 28 (8d6) Necrotic damage. The target's Hit Point maximum is reduced by an amount equal to the Necrotic damage taken, and the Sovereign regains that many Hit Points. The reduction lasts until the target finishes a Long Rest.

***Crimson Surge (Recharge 5–6).*** *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 19, each creature in a 60-foot Cone. *Failure:* 63 (14d8) damage of a type rolled once for the whole burst (1 Acid, 2 Cold, 3 Fire, 4 Lightning, 5 Thunder, 6 Force), and the target suffers a wild-magic surge effect. *Success:* Half damage only.

***Surge Step (Recharge 4–6).*** The Sovereign teleports up to 40 feet to an unoccupied space it can see.

***Surge Step (Costs 1 Action).*** The Sovereign teleports up to 20 feet to an unoccupied space it can see.

***Crimson Pulse (Costs 2 Actions).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 19, each creature in a 20-foot Emanation originating from the Sovereign. *Failure:* 22 (5d8) Force damage and the target has the Prone condition. *Success:* Half damage only.

**Loot.** *Heart of the Red Surge* (3,000 gp): a stone-hard, glowing organ that beats once an hour, a component for a major ritual of blood, storm or resurrection (or for a magic item of Legendary rarity at the DM's discretion). *Sovereign's Veinwork* (1,200 gp): glowing red filaments extracted from its body, wanted by artificers for rare armor. Its hoard holds heirlooms of a dozen families, a *Rod of Absorption*, a *Ring of Spell Storing* or a *Staff of the Magi*, and a plain, worn *Ring of Protection* it has never taken off.

**Lore.** The Sovereign is the one among them who remembers. It knows what it was, what it is, what it does and why it cannot stop, and it speaks of all this in a calm, tired voice. It treats those who come to destroy it as honored guests and a long-awaited relief.

**Ecology.** A tall humanoid wrapped in a slow storm of red light and blood-colored static, with an ancient face, a ruined crown and clothes older than the Catastrophe. It haunts the deepest scars of the world, such as the heart of a surge, the foot of a dead tower or the cellar of a cathedral, and gathers lesser vampires like weather gathers clouds. Its form is a guess, as it is a different shape in every account.

**Tactics.** It receives visitors, then ends the conversation when it has heard what it wished. It fights as a duelist and as a storm: Surge Step across the field, Crimson Surge on a clump, claws and bite on the strongest foe. Its legendary actions keep any single hero from pressing it. Speak its name and show it something from its life, and it will stop for a heartbeat. Bring a dead zone, running water or sunlight, and the fight is yours to win.

\page
## Scar-Knight
*Soldiers fused with their own weapon and armor mid-battle, still fighting a fight that ended for everyone else.*

On the day of the Catastrophe, there were battles going on. The soldiers in them did not stop. They were fused with their gear, caught in the middle of a swing or a charge, and they have not moved on. A Scar-Knight is a soldier whose war is still happening. They stand where they fell, march where they marched and fight whoever comes within reach.

**Armies that didn't stop.** A Scar-Knight is made in the moment: a soldier caught in a surge while holding a weapon and wearing armor merges with both. A battle with a high surge density may leave dozens. A Scar-Pikeman is a common infantryman. A Scar-Knight is a trained fighter. A Warlord's Echo is someone who was giving orders when it happened, and the others still obey.

**Ranks and orders.** They are strongest in formation. A Pikeman is sturdier in a line, a Knight follows the nearest Echo, and an Echo can direct fallen soldiers who haven't heard an order in ten years. Alone, a Scar-Knight just advances on whatever is closest. They cannot flee, and they cannot stop.

**Speaking to the past.** Occasionally a Scar-Knight hesitates, as if hearing something. A creature that names a detail of its battle, such as a regiment, a uniform or a commander's name, can pull it up short, and a scholar of military history is worth more than a fighter on some fields. Otherwise they speak only in fragments of orders.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Scar-Pikeman | 3 | Line infantry, reach and ranks |
| Scar-Knight | 5 | Heavy fused fighter |
| Warlord's Echo | 9 | Commander, rallies the fallen |

**Family rules.**
- **Endless Duel.** They must advance on and attack the nearest creature that can fight back, and cannot flee.
- **Fused Weapon.** Their weapons cannot be disarmed or sundered.
- **Frozen in the Last Stand.** Naming a detail of their battle gives them Disadvantage against that creature.
- **Flicker of the Person Within.** Badly hurt, they may hesitate for a turn.
- **Unique quirk.** The Pikeman is strongest in ranks, and weakest alone.

A pocket may hold a *Potion of Healing*, a pay ticket or a lock of hair in a locket.

**Lore.** The Pikemen were the line of every army: ordinary soldiers with ordinary names. They stand in rows when they stand in groups, and they stand in the road when they stand alone.

**Ecology.** A heavily armored figure whose pike, shield and armor have fused into its body and whose helm has become a face. It stands in the road or on a ridge where its unit fought, waiting for the next charge. Groups form ranks on instinct, and a lone Pikeman will take position beside any other Scar-Knight it sees.

\page
## Dead-Zone Beasts
*Predators and oozes born starving rather than overfed, actively hostile to magic rather than hungry for it.*

Where the Catastrophe overfed some places, it starved others. In a dead-magic zone, nothing magical lasts, and the animals that live in one learn quickly that magic is the enemy. Their bodies are tuned against it: they are dull to it, resistant to it, or ready to destroy it. They hunt casters, eat enchanted items and leave a trail of cold, quiet ground behind them.

**Where they live.** Dead zones are quiet places, and these creatures are quiet inside them. A pup born in one is a Deadglass Hound almost from birth. A Voidmaw is an ooze that has fed on magic items until it can no longer tell what else to eat. Vultures circle the dead places for years. A Voidheart is what a Voidmaw becomes after decades of feeding, and it grows a new dead zone wherever it goes.

**What they hunt.** They go for casters first, and for anything that glows. Their auras make spells harder to cast and harder to hold, so a party's usual tools fail. But they are starved, not stupid: an overpowered spell can overload one, and a party that fights without spells is only fighting an animal.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Deadglass Hound | 2 | Pack hunter, disrupts concentration |
| Voidmaw | 3 | Ooze that eats enchantments |
| Voidwing Vulture | 5 | Scavenger of the wounded |
| Voidheart | 11 | Spreading ooze, legendary actions |

**Family rules.**
- **Magic-Dull Aura.** Spells cast near them are made with Disadvantage, and Concentration is harder.
- **Resistant to spells.** The oozes and the vulture resist spell damage.
- **Overexposure.** A strong enough spell overloads their starved bodies and can Stun them.
- **Expanding dead zone.** The oldest of them make new dead zones just by living.
- **Unique quirk.** Magic hurts them more than ordinary harm, but a high-level spell may also stun them.

### Traits
***Magic-Dull Aura.*** A spell cast within 10 feet of the Vulture is made with Disadvantage on any attack roll it requires, and any Concentration saving throw made within that radius is made with Disadvantage.

***Carrion Sense.*** The Vulture senses the direction and rough distance of any creature with fewer than half its Hit Points remaining, and any corpse, within 1 mile.

***Dive Attack.*** If the Vulture moves at least 20 feet straight toward a target and then hits it with its Talons on the same turn, the target takes an extra 7 (2d6) Slashing damage.

***Overexposure.*** If the Vulture takes damage from a spell of 3rd level or higher, it must succeed on a DC 13 Constitution saving throw or take 9 (2d8) Force damage and have the Stunned condition until the end of its next turn, its magic-starved body violently overloaded.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Vulture makes one Beak attack and one Talons attack.

***Beak.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 10 (2d6 + 3) Piercing damage.

***Talons.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 7 (1d8 + 3) Slashing damage, and the target has the Grappled condition (escape DC 14). Until this grapple ends, the Vulture can't use its Talons on another target.

***Hollowing Shriek (Recharge 5–6).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 13, each creature in a 30-foot Cone. *Failure:* 14 (4d6) Force damage, and any effect on the target that requires Concentration ends. *Success:* Half damage only.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Hollow Quill* (50 gp): a long, pale flight-feather that is cold to the touch and makes a poor spell component and a superb scribe's pen for anything that must never be enchanted. *Null Beak* (75 gp): a dull, grey beak whose edge dispels minor magical effects on contact. Its roost holds the stripped belongings of the dead, who may have carried a *Potion of Resistance* (force) or a drained *Wand of Magic Detection*.

**Lore.** Pilgrims leaving the dead zones say the vultures arrive before the body is cold, and that they never seem to be hungry. They watch.

**Ecology.** A huge, ragged vulture with a bald, grey head, wings fringed in dull glass-like feathers and eyes like unlit lamps. It circles the dead-magic zones in slow spirals, descends on anything wounded or dying, and tears at magic items and spellbooks as readily as at flesh. It tends to nest in groups of two or three on cliffs and broken towers.

**Initiative**  :: --1 (9)

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** Voidheart can move through gaps as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Null Field.*** Any spell cast within 30 feet of Voidheart is made with disadvantage on any attack roll it requires, and any Concentration saving throw made within that radius is also made with disadvantage.

***Expanding Dead Zone.*** A single pass does little, but ground Voidheart crosses repeatedly slowly loses its capacity to hold magic; enough repeated exposure over weeks or months can create a new permanent dead-magic zone.

***Unmade (2/Day).*** If Voidheart fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** Voidheart makes two Slam attacks.

***Slam.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +9, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 23 (2d10 + 5) Bludgeoning damage plus 7 (2d6) Acid damage; if the target is wearing or wielding a magic item, that item must succeed on a DC 17 saving throw or lose 1d4 charges (or has a cumulative 10% chance of being destroyed, if it has no charges).

***Engulf (Recharge 4–6).*** Voidheart moves up to its Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks and attempts to engulf a Huge or smaller creature in its space. The target must succeed on a DC 18 Strength saving throw or be engulfed: it has the Restrained and Blinded conditions, can't breathe, and takes 14 (4d6) Acid damage at the start of each of its turns.

***Void Maw (Bonus Action, Recharge 5–6).*** Voidheart dispels one spell of 4th level or lower currently affecting a creature within 30 feet (no save) and regains 20 Hit Points.

### Legendary Actions (3/Turn)
{{legend Legendary Action Uses: 3. Immediately after another creature's turn, the Voidheart can expend a use to take one of the following actions. The voidheart regains expended uses at the start of each of its turns.}}

***Slam (Costs 1 Action).*** Voidheart makes one Slam attack.

***Ooze Forward (Costs 1 Action).*** Voidheart moves up to half its Speed.

***Void Pulse (Costs 2 Actions).*** Each creature within 15 feet of Voidheart must succeed on a DC 16 Constitution saving throw or take 13 (3d8) Force damage and have disadvantage on its next Concentration saving throw.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Voidheart Nucleus* (1,200 gp): a dense, black core that absorbs spells in a 30-foot radius; a once-in-a-lifetime component for anti-magic work. *Dead Slime Cask* (500 gp): a sealed cask of slime that creates a patch of dead magic when poured. Its remains include dozens of drained magical items, perhaps a drained *Staff of the Magi* and a *Rod of Absorption*.

**Lore.** Where the Voidheart goes, a dead zone grows. Cartographers update their maps accordingly, and the local priests are afraid.

**Ecology.** A huge, glistening mass of dark, dense ooze with a pulsing, lightless core, bigger than a house and still growing. It travels through dead zones, extending them, and eats magic wherever it finds it.

\page
## Guardians & Gentle Giants
*Creatures (plant, stone, or turtle) that settled into the role of protector rather than predator, and mean real harm only when provoked.*

Not everything the Catastrophe made is dangerous. Some of what stood up afterward simply stood in front of something else: a grove, a grave, a gate or a sleeping rift. They guard what was already there and defend it without malice. If you leave them alone, they leave you alone.

**What they guard.** Each is bound to a place. Chorus Roots grew from the roots of old graves. A Rift-Warden is a standing stone that woke beside a rift. A Groveheart Warden is a tree that decided to walk. The Chorusback Turtle carries a whole small ecosystem on its back. A Cairn Colossus is built from the stones of a burial mound, and what it guards is nearly always inside.

**The old rites.** Most can be asked to stand down by someone who knows the right gesture or word. The old rites were forgotten for a generation and are slowly being relearned, and priests, scholars and a few old farmers are suddenly very useful. A party that approaches with respect and a good Arcana or Religion check can walk past almost any of them.

**When they fight.** They fight rarely, but when they do, they are among the toughest creatures in this book, resistant to ordinary weapons and slow to anger, and impossible to flee from until they decide to stop. They are never the aggressor. If a fight starts, someone started it.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Chorus Root | 1 | Sings over a grove or grave |
| Rift-Warden | 2 | Stone sentinel of a rift |
| Groveheart Warden | 5 | Walking tree guarding a forest |
| Chorusback Turtle | 7 | Carries a small ecosystem |
| Cairn Colossus | 10 | Stone giant guarding a tomb |

**Family rules.**
- **Peaceable.** They will not attack unless what they guard is threatened.
- **Old Rite.** Many can be calmed by the right ritual, with a check.
- **Sturdy.** They are tough, resistant to ordinary weapons and slow to anger.
- **Unique quirk.** The Colossus can be weakened by prying a stone from its body.

**Lore.** It stands over graves and groves and sings low, layered notes through the night. Villagers say it keeps the dead company.

Behind the stone, if it was guarding a vault, lies the thing it guarded.

**Loot.** *Cairn-Heart* (800 gp): a smooth, warm river-stone buried deep in its chest, a focus for protection and boundary magic and a legendary component for a ward that lasts a hundred years. *Mortar of the Ages* (200 gp): a pouch of fine grey dust that, mixed with water and applied to a wall, repairs it as the *mending* cantrip once. The thing it guards is almost always inside its cairn: a tomb, a hoard, a bound spirit, or a *Staff of Power*.

**Lore.** Every old people built a cairn for their dead, and every old people knew the dead needed guarding. The Catastrophe woke the guards. The old stories say you do not fight a Colossus; you apologise, and you leave.

**Ecology.** A towering heap of fitted stones in the rough shape of a person, with moss, lichen and the occasional wildflower growing in the cracks. It stands among the other cairns on a ridge, hilltop or moor, and does not move for decades at a time. It stands up slowly, and with a grinding sound, when something is wrong.
\page
## Rift-Born Spirits
*Not mutated flesh at all: beings condensed directly out of raw rift-energy or unresolved grief, closer to weather than to animal.*

When a rift opens, magic spills out, and sometimes it keeps going. Rift-Born Spirits are what happens when that spill takes a shape. Some are curious, like the Rift Wisp. Some are sorrowful, like the Rift Wraith and the Mourning Gale. None are made of meat, and none can be killed in a conventional way.

**What they are made of.** A Wisp forms from simple spill and needs no reason to exist. A Wraith needs one: a death, a loss, a secret, something heavy enough to hold the spill in a shape. A Mourning Gale needs a grief large enough to move weather. The heavier the reason, the larger the spirit, and the longer it stays.

**What they want.** Spirits are what they are bound to. They are not evil, and most want something that can be found, given or finished. Wisps mimic what they see and hear. Wraiths and Gales are bound to a specific thing and will only fight if it is disturbed. Settle the matter, with a burial, a returned keepsake or an apology, and they fade. A party that fights one has usually missed the point.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Rift Wisp | 1/8 | Curious flicker of light |
| Rift Wraith | 2 | Bound to a grave or keepsake |
| Mourning Gale | 5 | A grief large enough to move weather |

**Family rules.**
- **Unfinished Business.** A Wraith or Gale is tied to a site, an object or a wrong, and fades if it is resolved.
- **Grief-Touched.** They will not attack unless what they guard is disturbed.
- **Incorporeal.** They pass through objects and creatures.
- **Unique quirk.** The Gale is slowed in sealed rooms, and the Wisp will never fight.

***Flicker Step.*** As a Bonus Action, the wisp teleports up to 20 feet to an unoccupied space it can see.

***Curious Mimicry.*** The wisp can perfectly mimic simple sounds and lights it has heard or seen (a whistle, a lantern's glow, a voice's cadence) — mischief, not malice, is always the intent.

**Loot.** It leaves nothing when it fades, only a faint spark of light that lingers for a breath. A person who catches that spark in a bottle holds a *Rift Spark* (15 gp), a one-use light that never burns out until it is opened. Wisps do not carry anything.

**Lore.** Children chase them and are told not to. Old travelers say a wisp mimicking your voice is only being friendly, and a wisp mimicking someone you lost is only trying to be.

**Ecology.** A hand-sized mote of drifting light that darts, flickers and mimics sounds and glows. It forms from loose rift-energy at the edge of a rift, drifts in small groups, and vanishes at dawn or if frightened. It has no interest in fighting.

***Incorporeal Movement.*** The wraith can move through other creatures and objects as if they were Difficult Terrain. It takes 5 (1d10) Force damage if it ends its turn inside an object.

***Unfinished Business.*** The wraith is bound to a specific site, object, or unresolved wrong (determined by the GM — an unburied body, a stolen keepsake, an unspoken apology). If that condition is genuinely resolved, the wraith fades peacefully and can't be provoked into combat again.

***Grief-Touched.*** The wraith won't attack unless a living creature tries to take, damage, or disturb the specific thing tied to its death.

***Chilling Touch.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +4, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 10 (3d6) Necrotic damage, and the target has disadvantage on the next saving throw it makes against being Frightened.

**Loot.** *Grief Shard* (80 gp): a cold sliver of crystallized sorrow, a component for rituals of mourning, peace or *speak with dead*. When its business is resolved it leaves behind the thing that bound it, such as a keepsake, a letter or a ring, which is often worth more to the right person than to a fence.

**Lore.** It is not hunting you; it is waiting for someone to finish what it couldn't. Most are laid to rest by a burial, an apology or a return of what was taken.

**Ecology.** A translucent, humanoid figure of pale light and shadow that hovers near a grave, a keepsake or a place of death. It rarely moves far, has a faint, sorrowful voice and is hostile only to those who disturb what it guards.

|Str|6|--2|--2|Dex|18|+4|+7|Con|14|+2|+2|
|Int|6|--2|--2|Wis|14|+2|+5|Cha|16|+3|+3|

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks; Lightning, Thunder
**Immunities**  :: Exhaustion, Grappled, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned, Prone, Restrained
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 12
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 5 (XP 1,800; PB +3)

### Traits
***Incorporeal Movement.*** The Gale can move through other creatures and objects as if they were Difficult Terrain. It takes 5 (1d10) Force damage if it ends its turn inside an object.

***Unfinished Business.*** The Gale is bound to a specific site, object, or unresolved wrong (determined by the GM: a battlefield with no burial, a drowned village, a promise not kept). If that condition is genuinely resolved, the Gale fades peacefully and can't be provoked into combat again.

***Grief-Touched.*** The Gale won't attack unless a living creature tries to take, damage, or disturb the specific thing tied to its death.

***Windless.*** In a sealed room, or inside the area of a Silence spell, the Gale's Fly Speed is 10 feet and it has Disadvantage on attack rolls.

### Actions
***Multiattack (Once Hostile).*** The Gale makes two Grieving Gust attacks.

***Grieving Gust.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +7, reach 15 ft. *Hit:* 13 (2d8 + 4) Cold damage.

***Wail of the Gale (Recharge 5–6).*** *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 14, each creature in a 30-foot Emanation originating from the Gale. *Failure:* 18 (4d8) Psychic damage, the target is pushed up to 15 feet away from the Gale, and it has the Frightened condition for 1 minute (it can't willingly move closer to the Gale while Frightened this way). *Success:* Half damage only.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Bottled Gale* (150 gp): a stoppered bottle in which something sighs and moves, once opened it releases a 30-foot gust that scatters light objects and extinguishes flames. *Grief-Cloth* (40 gp): a thin veil that is always cool and a little damp, used in funerary rites. The thing it was bound to, the site's treasure or keepsake, remains when the Gale fades, and is often worth more to the right buyer than to a pawnshop.

**Lore.** Fishermen call it the widow's wind; soldiers, the reveille. It is the same spirit: a grief that grew large enough to move the weather. If it can be quieted, the wind drops, and the sea goes flat for the first time in years.

**Ecology.** A tall, translucent column of moving air with the faint outline of a face, a hand or a coat, trailing leaves, ash or spray depending on the ground it haunts. It hovers over a grave, a battlefield, a flooded village or a wrecked ship, and the weather around it is always a little wrong. It does not hunt, it does not feed, and it does not leave.
\page
## Swarms
*Many small bodies acting, to greater or lesser degree, as one.*

The Catastrophe changed the small things, too. A moth, a locust, a starling or a bee is a minor thing alone, but a thousand of them acting as one is something else. Swarms are among the most common Catastrophe-changed creatures, and among the easiest to meet by accident.

**One mind, many bodies.** A swarm has one mind, if you can call it that: a shared attention, a shared hunger, a shared fear. It responds to light, warmth, sound and magic in ways that look intelligent but aren't. A Starling Chorus can repeat a voice; it cannot say what the voice means. Kill enough of the bodies and the mind thins, which is why every swarm hits much softer once it is half gone.

**Handling a swarm.** Each has a trick that works, and the trick is usually ordinary: smoke for bees, cold for locusts, a bell for starlings, a lantern left in the wrong place for moths. Swarms don't plan, so a party that knows the trick can turn a deadly fight into an annoying one. A party that doesn't can be driven off a road by a thousand moths.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Rift Moth Swarm | 1/2 | Drawn to magic, dazzles |
| Cinderwing Locust Swarm | 1 | Burns crops and grain |
| Starling Chorus | 2 | Mimics voices, lures travelers |
| Chime-Bee Swarm | 3 | Lulls with a drowsy hum |

**Family rules.**
- **Swarm.** They share space with creatures and resist ordinary weapons.
- **Weakened when hurt.** Damage drops sharply when the swarm is at half HP or fewer.
- **Spell-drawn.** Several are attracted to casters and magic items.
- **Unique quirk.** The Locusts fear cold, the Bees fear smoke and the Starlings are thrown by loud sounds.

The swarm holds no treasure of its own, but its dusty nest sometimes shelters a lost coin pouch or a *Spell Scroll* (cantrip).

**Lore.** Mages joke that a moth swarm is the cheapest detect magic there is: wherever it gathers, someone has cast something. Less amusing is what happens to the caster.

**Ecology.** A cloud of palm-sized moths with shimmering, rainbow wings that leave a haze of glittering dust behind them. They roost in crevices near rifts and drift toward spellcasters and magic items. Left alone, they drift away by dawn.

**Lore.** They learn voices and use them. Travelers lost in the forest swear they heard their own names called from the trees, and followed them.

The hive is a hollow tree or an old bell-tower, and it may hold a *Potion of Healing* and a few coins stuck in the wax.

**Lore.** Keepers of the old orchards say the bees came first to the bells, then to the people, and that they have not stopped humming since. A hive that goes quiet is a hive that has been disturbed.

**Ecology.** A swarm of palm-sized bees with silver-and-gold bodies that ring faintly as they fly, building honeycombs on bells, in belfries and in hollow trees. They feed on flowers lit by rift-light, hum in a low, drowsy chord, and are driven off by smoke and cold. The beekeepers who know them work at dawn, in wet cloth and gloves.
\page
## Small Omens & Curiosities
*Low-threat, high-flavor foundlings: more useful as world texture, warning signs, and roleplay hooks than as fights.*

Most of what the Catastrophe changed is small. A glowing owl, a ringing snail, a crow that knows where the dead are. These creatures are not dangerous, and most people love them. They are omens: signs that something is wrong, or that something is right, or that something is coming.

**Reading the signs.** None of them is trying to warn anyone, but the effect is the same. The owl hoots at scars, the crow gathers at deaths, the wren falls silent at dead zones, the moth flies toward any flame. A traveler who learns the signs can read the land as easily as a map, and a guide who knows them is worth the pay. In the first years, towns kept caged wrens and owls for just this reason.

**Using them.** They are good for scenery, for hooks and for small decisions: a party that follows the crow finds the body, one that follows the snail finds a safe place to sleep. They die easily, and a party that kills one has probably given up something useful. The Rift Tick is the exception: its bite is mild and its consequences are not.

| Creature | CR | Omen |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Glasswing Owl | 1/8 | Hoots near active scar-sites |
| Chime Snail | 1/8 | Lulls those who rest near it |
| Rift Tick | 1/8 | Slowly mutates its host |
| Quiet-Song Wren | 1/8 | Falls silent near dead zones |
| Mirrorwing Moth | 1/4 | Drawn to any flame |
| Gravehollow Crow | 1/4 | Gathers at the newly dead |
| Glimmerback Toad | 1/4 | Leaves a glowing film |

**Family rules.**
- **Peaceable.** Almost none will fight unless cornered.
- **Sign-bearing.** Each carries one sign that a smart party can read.
- **Fragile.** They die easily and are best used as scenery, warnings and hooks.
- **Unique quirk.** The Tick's slow mutation is the only thing here that can change a character.

\page

### Traits
***Glasswing Glow.*** The owl's wings are faintly translucent and bioluminescent, shedding Dim Light in a 10-foot radius. As a Bonus Action, it can suppress or restore this glow at will.

***Silent Flight.*** The owl has advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks made while flying.

***Scar-Sense.*** The owl can sense the direction and rough distance of any active scar-site or major unstable-magic event within 1 mile of it. When within 300 feet of one, it becomes visibly agitated — its glow flickers and it hoots continuously — whether or not anyone is around to notice.

***Peaceable Nature.*** The owl has no wish to fight and will not attack a creature that hasn't attacked it, its roost-mates, or its nest.

### Actions
***Peck.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 1 Piercing damage. The owl uses this only in genuine self-defense, and only after every other option has failed.

**Loot.** *Glasswing Feather* (30 gp): a translucent, softly glowing feather that sheds Dim Light for a day and shimmers near unstable magic. *Owl Pellet* (5 gp): a pellet of bone and glass that a naturalist or wizard might value. It carries no other remains. Harming one is considered very bad luck.

**Lore.** The owls hoot nonstop before a rift opens. Some villages keep one in a cage and take the hooting as a warning to flee.

**Ecology.** A small owl with translucent, glowing wings that sheds a faint light as it glides silently through the night. It roosts in hollow trees and ruins near scars and shows distress near unstable magic. It is peaceful and curious.

### Traits
***Chiming Shell.*** The snail's shell rings faintly with every movement, audible up to 30 feet. It can never benefit from being unheard, but the sound is gentle rather than alarming — low and musical, closer to a distant wind chime than a warning bell.

***Lulling Tone.*** A creature that falls asleep or takes a short rest within 15 feet of a resting Chime Snail's chiming has advantage on any Constitution saving throw made to avoid being woken by nonviolent disturbances during that rest.

***Withdraw (Bonus Action).*** The snail pulls into its shell, reducing its Speed to 0 but gaining a +4 bonus to AC until the start of its next turn.

### Actions
***Nip.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +2, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 1 Piercing damage.

}}

**Loot.** *Chime Shell* (15 gp): a spiral shell that rings softly when moved and lulls those who sleep near it. A shell kept in a pack gives a small comfort on a bad night. The snail itself is not edible and carries nothing.

**Lore.** Travelers trust the sound. When a chime goes quiet, they say, something is wrong.

**Ecology.** A thumb-sized snail with a pearly, bell-like shell that rings with every movement. It lives on damp stone, mossy walls and cave mouths, is slow and harmless, and withdraws at the first sign of danger.

{{monster,frame
## Rift Tick
*Tiny Monstrosity, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 12
**HP**  :: 4 (1d4 + 2)
**Speed**  :: 10 ft., climb 10 ft.

**Initiative**  :: +2 (12)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|3|--4|--4|Dex|14|+2|+2|Con|12|+1|+1|
|Int|1|--5|--5|Wis|9|--1|--1|Cha|2|--4|--4|

**Senses**  :: Blindsight 10 ft., Passive Perception 9
**CR**  :: 1/8 (XP 25; PB +2)

### Traits
***Latch On.*** If the tick's Bite hits, it attaches to the target instead of letting go. While attached, it can't be targeted separately from its host (attacks against the host can be redirected to it if the attacker chooses), and it automatically hits with Bite each round without needing to attack again. The host or another creature can remove it with an action (no roll needed), or the host can take an action to make a DC 10 Strength (Athletics) check to pull it off.

***Slow Mutation.*** At the end of each full day the tick remains attached, its host must succeed on a Constitution saving throw (DC 11, increasing by 1 for each previous day attached) or take on a minor, GM-determined mutation — cosmetic at first, but not guaranteed to stay that way the longer it's left in place. Removing the tick stops further checks but doesn't reverse a mutation already gained.

### Actions
***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +4, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 1 Piercing damage, and the tick attaches (see Latch On).

}}

**Loot.** *Rift Ichor* (30 gp): glowing blood from a fed tick, a volatile reagent for alchemy that mutates small animals. The tick itself carries nothing. A sample kept in a sealed vial is prized by scholars of the Catastrophe.

**Lore.** The bite is nothing; the days afterward are the problem. Healers check travelers for ticks before they let them into a village.

**Ecology.** A bean-sized tick with a faintly glowing abdomen that waits on grass and branches near rifts. It latches on to any warm creature, feeds slowly, and passes a little of the rift's energy into its host.

***Quiet-Song.*** The wren sings constantly, audible out to 60 feet. When it is within 120 feet of a dead-magic zone, an *antimagic field*, a Voidmaw, or another creature with a Magic-Dull Aura, it falls silent and stays silent for as long as it remains there.

***Magic-Hearing.*** The wren has Advantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks to detect spellcasting within 120 feet.

***Silence-Sleep.*** While the wren is inside the area of a *silence* spell, it has the Unconscious condition until it leaves.

***Peaceable Nature.*** The wren has no wish to fight and won't attack unless cornered.

\column

**Loot.** *Wren Feather* (10 gp): a tiny, brown-and-gold feather that stays warm for a week and hums faintly near magic; a pilgrim's token. *Song-Bone* (5 gp): a fine, hollow bone that makes a clear, thin whistle. The nest holds nothing but moss, thread and an occasional lost earring.

**Lore.** Every caravan master carries a wren in a cage or wears its feather in a hat. When the song stops, the old guides say, it is time to turn back.

**Ecology.** A thumb-sized brown wren with gold-tipped wings and a bright, trilling voice that carries remarkably far. It lives in hedgerows, ruins and caves, nests in the same place year after year, and sings from dawn to dusk. Dead-magic zones silence it so completely that people have used wrens to find the edges of them.
\page
## Waters of the Catastrophe
*Aquatic and amphibious mutations found along flooded rift coastlines and storm-touched seas.*

Water carries magic farther and faster than air. When the Catastrophe struck, the rivers, coasts and shallows took the worst of it. What lives in them now is strange and beautiful: eels with lanterns in their heads, skates that crackle, crabs with bells for shells. They are changed, but they are not all dangerous.

**The flooded coasts.** The sea holds a surge for a long time, so aquatic creatures are exposed constantly, and each generation is changed more than the last. A flooded cellar can host a Tideglass Eel within a few years. A Glimmerfin Skate lies in sand and silt, a Tidebell Crab in rock pools, and the Stormtide Ray needs open water and a storm to follow. The Prismhide Octopus hides in wrecks and takes on the color of whatever it is sitting in.

**Light and sound.** Almost everything here hunts, hides or warns with light and sound. The Eel's lantern draws prey. The Crab's bell alerts the colony. The Octopus is drawn to any bright thing. Divers and sailors learn the lights by heart: which are lures, which are warnings, and which mean nothing at all.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Glimmerfin Skate | 1/2 | Shocks what steps on it |
| Tidebell Crab | 1/2 | Rings a warning |
| Tideglass Eel | 1 | Lures prey with a lantern |
| Stormtide Ray | 4 | Rides storms, flies and swims |
| Prismhide Octopus | 5 | Camouflaged, drawn to light |

**Family rules.**
- **Amphibious.** Several breathe both air and water.
- **Light and sound.** Many hunt or hide with light, and some respond to bells or storms.
- **Peaceable.** The smaller ones fight only when cornered.
- **Unique quirk.** The Eel is useless in bright light, and the Octopus cannot resist a lantern.

**Lore.** Sailors tell of a light in the water that is not a ship. Those who follow it don't come back.

Its den holds wreck salvage, perhaps a *Potion of Water Breathing* or a *Cloak of the Manta Ray*.

**Lore.** Divers say it takes the color of the wreck it's sitting in, and that they know a particular octopus by the color of the lost ship it wears. A few claim they've been looked at by one for a very long time before it moved.

**Ecology.** A great, soft-bodied octopus the size of a rowing boat, with skin that ripples through every color and a single large eye that watches everything. It lives in sunken ships, caves and reefs near rifts, reaches out to anything bright or shiny, and vanishes into the stone when frightened.
\page
## Predators & Hunters
*Beast mutations without a family of their own: ambush hunters, apex chargers, and everything in between.*

Not every changed animal belongs to a family. Some are simply what you get when an ordinary predator spends a season in a bad place. A mole that tunnels through ash, a mantis that eats spells, a lynx with poisonous quills, a lion with a mane of static. They hunt like their ordinary cousins, but with a twist.

**One trick each.** What sets these animals apart is a single, very specific ability: the Cindermole for footsteps, the Mantis for spellcasters, the Packlord for a pack. That specificity is also their weakness. The Lion's mane fails when soaked, the Rhino can't turn, the Boar can be led off by a stronger scent, and the Packlord is helpless without its pack. Hunters who survive the longest are the ones who learn the trick.

**By terrain.** Most are tied to a type of ground. Moles and newts like ash and warm stone, mantises and lynxes like thickets, stags and boars like forest, bears like the high cold, lions and rhinos like open plains. Meeting one outside its terrain is rare, and usually means something else has gone wrong in the place it came from.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:-:|:---|
| Cindermole | 1 | Ambushes from beneath ash |
| Emberback Newt | 1 | Warm-stone skulker |
| Manabloom Mantis | 2 | Eats spell slots |
| Ridgeback Boar | 2 | Blind charger |
| Ashfang Packlord | 3 | Pack leader |
| Chime-Web Spider | 3 | Silences with resonant silk |
| Thistleback Lynx | 3 | Quill-armored ambusher |
| Glass Antler Stag | 3 | Light-scattering charger |
| Featherstorm Harpy | 4 | Dive-bomber with a shriek |
| Amberfrost Bear | 5 | Encases prey in amber-ice |
| Duskhorn Rhino | 6 | Unstoppable, hard to turn |
| Surgemane Lion | 7 | Static-maned apex hunter |

**Family rules.**
- **One trick.** Each has a single signature ability that a smart party can work around.
- **Charging.** Several are most dangerous when they get a straight run.
- **Environment.** Most are tied to a type of terrain, and weaker outside it.
- **Unique quirk.** The Lion's mane fails when soaked, and the Packlord is weak alone.

**Lore.** Farmers on the ash plains tell their children to walk heavily, so the moles know where you are.

**Ecology.** A heavy, ash-grey mole the size of a dog, with huge digging claws and a faintly glowing nose. It tunnels beneath ash fields and burned ground, ambushes anything that walks above, and is deafened by loud noises.

**Lore.** Mages hate it for a good reason: it takes the spell, not the life. A caster that meets it is left slumped and drained, still alive and quite embarrassed.

### Traits
***Web Walker.*** The spider ignores movement restrictions caused by webbing.

***Chime Silk.*** A creature Restrained by the spider's webbing can't cast spells that require a Verbal component, the resonant threads warping any spoken sound into meaningless noise.

***Resonance-Bound.*** If the spider is exposed to a sudden, dominant loud sound (a horn, a bell, a thunderclap) it must succeed on a DC 13 Constitution saving throw or have disadvantage on attack rolls until the end of its next turn, its hunting sense thrown off by the competing noise.

### Actions
***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 10 (2d6 + 3) Piercing damage plus 7 (2d6) Poison damage.

***Chime Web (Recharge 5–6).*** *Ranged Attack Roll:* +6, range 30/60 ft., one creature. *Hit:* The target has the Restrained condition (escape DC 13) and is subject to Chime Silk while restrained.

}}

**Loot.** *Chime Silk* (90 gp per bundle): a bundle of resonant silk that rings faintly and muffles speech, a component for silencing items. *Spider Venom Gland* (50 gp): a poison source. Its web holds cocooned victims and their gear, which may include a *Potion of Healing* or a *Spell Scroll*.

**Lore.** Travelers report the sound of a distant bell before they found the web. Those who answer it are not seen again.

**Ecology.** A Large spider with a pale body and legs that ring like fine glass as it walks. It spins vast webs across ruined halls and canyon mouths, feeds on whatever stumbles in, and is thrown off by loud noises.

**Lore.** It is not a harpy in the old sense, only a bird that learned to scream. People who have been deafened by one say they never heard it coming.

**Ecology.** A Large, grey-feathered bird-woman with long talons and a hollow-boned frame, nesting on cliffs and ruined towers in stormy country. It dives from the clouds and screams to stun its prey.

Its lair holds the bones of previous meals and the gear of unlucky hunters, perhaps including a *Potion of Resistance* (lightning) or a *Wand of Binding*.

**Lore.** The old stories of lions had a mane of fire. Hunters in the dry plains say the stories were right about the mane and wrong about the fire. It cracks and sparks when it hunts, and the thunder follows its roar by a heartbeat.

**Ecology.** A huge, tawny lion with a thick mane that crackles with blue-white static and eyes like polished copper. It rules the grasslands and open ridges, hunts mostly at dusk, and drinks only when the sky is clear. A lion does not abide wet weather; its mane goes limp and its temper short.
\page
## The Glass Waste
*Where the source burned the sand to glass, and the sand kept the shape of it.*

The Glass Waste is a desert that was made in an afternoon. On the day of the Catastrophe, the raw magic of the broken source arced across the dry country in the south. It struck the sand and the salt flats and left them glass: miles of fused plain, long tubes of lightning-melted rock and shelves that ring underfoot like bells. Elsewhere, dead-magic basins drank the water from the oases, one by one, until the springs ran dry and the wadis cracked. What is left is bright, hard and silent, and everything in it is shaped by two facts: there is almost no water, and there is magic enough to make a mirage you can walk into.

**Thirst is the first predator.** Water is the one resource that matters in the Glass Waste, and the creatures here have all arranged themselves around it. Some find it, some carry it, some steal it and some are made by its absence. A Mirage Hare will lead you to a spring. A Cistern Tortoise is a spring. A Thirstmaw is the reason you should keep yours hidden. Caravan-masters speak of water the way sailors speak of weather: with respect, and a little fear.

**What the heat remembers.** In the glare of midday, the Glass Waste shows things that are not there. Some of it is simple refraction, and some is the Catastrophe, still replaying across the glass. Old travellers learn to tell the two apart. The Haze Mourner is the worst of it, a thing that wears the shimmer like a coat and shows each traveller whoever they lost. At night, the glass goes dark and the voices stop, but the Fulgurite Wyrm wakes when the storms come.

**Harmless and harmful.** Not everything here wants you dead. The hare and the tortoise are the reason caravans survive, and killing one is a crime in most desert towns. The other three are what the caravans are afraid of. All five are products of the same place, and the same principle holds: when you know what a creature wants, you know how to live next to it.

| Creature | CR | Disposition |
|:---|:---:|:---|
| Mirage Hare | 1/8 | Harmless, leads to water |
| Cistern Tortoise | 1/2 | Harmless, carries water |
| Thirstmaw | 3 | Ambush predator, drains water |
| Haze Mourner | 6 | Mirage hunter, charms |
| Fulgurite Wyrm | 10 | Storm-fed burrower |

**Family rules.**
- **Water is the currency.** Every creature here is driven by water, whether it finds it, carries it or takes it.
- **Heat and glass.** Their powers depend on sun, glare and fused sand, and fail in shade, cold or the dark.
- **Dead-magic weakness.** Dead-magic zones strip the powers of the creatures here, which makes them a refuge for the traveller and a trap for the tortoise.

### Traits

***Quenched.*** If the hare is splashed with at least a pint of water, it can't use Heat Haze for 1 hour.

### Actions
***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 1 Piercing damage.

**Loot.** *Hare's Foot* (5 gp): a flickering charm that gives Advantage on one Wisdom (Survival) check to find water per day. *Haze-Fur* (10 gp): shimmering fur, sold as a curiosity.

**Lore.** Caravan-masters say a Mirage Hare is the one animal that has never lied to them. It shows you where the water is, plus an extra one or two that aren't there, because it cannot help itself.

**Ecology.** A small, sandy hare that shivers with heat shimmer, looking like two or three hares at once. It lives in burrows under glass shelves, drinks at dawn, and follows travellers hoping for scraps. It is shy and harmless.

**Tactics.** It flees at the first sign of trouble. Leave a little water on a flat rock and wait. A splash of water ends its trick for an hour, and not every shimmer it makes is real.

## Cistern Tortoise
*Large Monstrosity, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 16 (natural armor)
**HP**  :: 30 (4d10 + 8)

**Initiative**  :: --1 (9)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|16|+3|+3|Dex|8|--1|--1|Con|14|+2|+2|
|Int|3|--4|--4|Wis|12|+1|+1|Cha|6|--2|--2|

**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 11
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 1/2 (XP 100; PB +2)

### Traits
***Cistern Shell.*** The tortoise's shell holds up to 50 gallons of clean, cool water and refills at a rate of 1 gallon per hour, even in dry places. A creature that approaches calmly can draw water from the spout by succeeding on a DC 11 Wisdom (Animal Handling) check; on a failure, the tortoise withdraws into its shell.

***Dead-Magic Drain.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the tortoise's shell no longer refills and loses all its water within 1 minute. The tortoise gains 1 level of Exhaustion for each hour it remains there.

### Actions
***Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 6 (1d6 + 3) Piercing damage.

### Bonus Actions
***Withdraw.*** The tortoise pulls into its shell. Until it uses this action again, its AC is 20, its Speed is 0 and it has Disadvantage on Dexterity saving throws. It leaves its shell as a Bonus Action.

}}

**Loot.** *Cistern-Plate* (60 gp): a scute that keeps liquids cool and fresh for a week as a lid. *Waterhorn* (30 gp): the small spout, which holds a gallon. The water is the real reward.

**Lore.** The first was a pet, they say, and it drank from a spell cast to fill a well. The well is gone. The spell remains in its shell.

**Ecology.** A Large tortoise with a high, dome-shaped shell patterned like a cracked riverbed and a bronze spout where the neck meets the carapace. It plods between oases and ruins in slow circuits. It attacks only if attacked.

**Tactics.** It withdraws first, then bites if cornered. Treat it as a moving spring: approach calmly from the front and make an Animal Handling check. Keep it out of dead-magic zones, where its shell drains.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Thirstmaw makes one Parching Bite attack and one Tail Slap attack.

***Parching Bite.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 10 (2d6 + 3) Piercing damage plus 5 (2d4) Necrotic damage. *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 12. *Failure:* The target gains 1 level of Exhaustion (maximum 3 from this attack), and can't remove it with rest until it has drunk at least a gallon of water.

***Tail Slap.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +5, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 8 (1d10 + 3) Bludgeoning damage.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Parched Hide* (100 gp): a cracked, leathery skin which, treated, makes a waterskin that keeps water cold and never leaks. *Dry Fang* (45 gp): a long tooth that glows faintly in the presence of water, and is wanted by diviners and well-diggers. Its stomach may hold the dry remains of its last victim's gear, perhaps a *Potion of Healing* or a *Cloak of Protection*.

**Lore.** The Thirstmaw is what is left of a desert lizard that lived through the drying of the Gilded Basin, when a dead-magic zone swallowed three oases in a week. It has been thirsty ever since.

**Ecology.** A lean, sand-coloured lizard the size of a horse, with a wide, wrinkled mouth, hollow cheeks and a tongue that is always wet. It sleeps under sand and rocks and ambushes anything that smells of water. It prefers dead-magic basins and drained riverbeds, and never drinks its fill.

**Tactics.** It waits until its target is close and attacks the creature with the most water, so the party's water-carrier is in danger. Its Parching Bite wears down the party over several rounds. Throwing a full waterskin 30 feet away or pouring one out draws it to the puddle for a turn, which is a good time to run or hit it. After that it learns, and stops falling for it.

***Heat-Haze Form.*** Ranged attack rolls against the Haze Mourner made from more than 30 feet away have Disadvantage.

***Heat-Bound.*** While the Haze Mourner is in shade, magical darkness, rain, or an area below 40 degrees Fahrenheit, it loses Heat-Haze Form and can't use Mourning Mirage or Shimmer Step.

***Mirrored Truth.*** A creature within 5 feet of a target charmed by Mourning Mirage can use an action to hold up a mirror or polished metal, so the target sees its own face. The target repeats the save with Advantage.

***Multiattack.*** The Haze Mourner makes two Rake attacks.

***Rake.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +7, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 11 (2d6 + 4) Slashing damage.

While Charmed, it moves toward the vision on its turn by the route it believes is safest, ignores hazards, and takes no actions. It repeats the save at the end of each of its turns and whenever it takes damage from a source other than the Haze Mourner, ending the effect on a success. *Success:* The target is immune to Mourning Mirage for 24 hours.

\column

**Loot.** *Mourner's Veil* (400 gp): a shimmering veil of dried haze that, worn, makes the wearer appear as a trick of the heat to creatures more than 30 feet away (Disadvantage on ranged attacks against them in sunlight). *Heartless Mirror* (250 gp): a small shard of silvered glass, which shows the viewer someone they miss. It is a cruel keepsake. Few would buy one, and fewer would sell it. It carries no other remains.

**Lore.** In the first desert summer after the Catastrophe, travellers on the Glass Road began walking into the dunes in groups of two or three. Those that came back said they had seen a person they had lost. None of them have wanted to say who.

**Ecology.** A tall, hollow-eyed predator with long limbs and a face that is always slightly out of focus. It is almost transparent in the heat, a figure made from shimmer. It stalks the middle of the day on open dunes and glass flats, sleeps in the shade of dead cairns, and is never seen at night. It speaks softly in the voices of those people it has taken.

**Tactics.** It uses Mourning Mirage on the nearest target and lets the victim wander into the dunes while it works through the rest. It cannot hold a mirage in shade, in rain or at night, so the best approach is to retreat into darkness or cold, or to break the spell with a mirror held up to the victim's face. Cold damage hurts it badly, and a *ray of frost* or an ice-filled waterskin does far more than a sword.

**Loot.** *Fulgurite Rod* (800 gp): a fused glass tube as long as an arm that glows when lightning is near. A wizard can use it as the focus for a *lightning bolt* or *call lightning*. *Storm-Glass Plate* (500 gp): a smooth, tinted pane of shattered armor that can be cut into windows, lenses or a shield (AC +2, vulnerable to Thunder). Deep in its tunnels may be a *Rod of the Pact Keeper* or an *Amulet of Proof against Detection and Location*.

**Lore.** The Wyrm of the Brass Dunes is not named. It is a lightning bolt that struck for seven days and nights during the Catastrophe, burning a tunnel through the sand as it went. When it stopped, the tunnel had a mouth.

**Ecology.** A Huge serpentine creature made of translucent tubes of fused glass, with veins of white light pulsing along its length. It tunnels under dunes and glass flats, rising to hunt when lightning strikes within a mile. It sleeps in its own glass tunnels, and the smell of ozone follows it. It is solitary, territorial and always slightly dazed from the storm it came from.

**Tactics.** It ambushes from below, usually opening with Fulgurite Lance on a line of travellers, and follows with Bite and Tail Slam. Do not cast lightning: it heals it, and recharges its Lance. Thunder is the answer, because it is Vulnerable to Thunder; a *thunderwave* or a shout from a bard shatters its plates, while a big hit sprays glass into the party. A storm approaching from the horizon is a warning.

## The Cataclysm's Echo
*Not a family. Not really a mutation, either. Everything else in this bestiary is something the Catastrophe changed. These are closer to the Catastrophe itself, still happening.*

**Happening once, happening again.** The Cataclysm's Echo is the day of the Catastrophe made flesh: a wound in reality that has not finished opening. The Cataclysm's Refrain is what the world does about it. When a scar is deep enough and a surge strikes it hard enough, the day does not simply echo, it repeats. The same flash, the same faces, the same final words, over and over, with a little more of the world pulled in each time. The Refrain does not move toward anything. It hangs where the worst surges recur, and recites.

| Creature | CR | Role |
|:---|:---:|:---|
| The Cataclysm's Echo | 15 | The wound itself |
| The Cataclysm's Refrain | 20 | The wound, repeating |

**Family rules.**
- **Unstable reality.** They teleport, reroll and rewrite what is near them.
- **They heal on death.** A death near them is food.
- **Dead-magic weakness.** A dead-magic zone strips most of what they can do.

\page

### Traits
***Living Rift.*** At the start of the Echo's turn, roll a d8 and apply the corresponding effect in a 20-foot radius centered on it (GM's discretion or a prepared wild-magic surge table) — reality around it simply doesn't hold still.

***Echo of Everything.*** Whenever a creature within 60 feet of the Echo dies, it regains 15 Hit Points.

***Unmaking Constant (3/Day).*** If the Echo fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Echo makes three Rift Lash attacks.

***Rift Lash.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +10, reach 15 ft. *Hit:* 19 (3d8 + 5) Force damage, and the target is teleported up to 10 feet in a random direction (GM's choice or roll a d8 for facing).

***Unmaking Pulse (Recharge 5–6).*** Each creature within 30 feet of the Echo must make a DC 18 Constitution saving throw, taking 45 (10d8) Force damage on a failure or half as much on a success. A creature reduced below half its Hit Point maximum by this damage also suffers a wild-magic surge effect, regardless of the save's outcome.

***Rift Step (Bonus Action).*** The Echo teleports up to 60 feet to an unoccupied space it can see.

### Legendary Actions (3/Turn)
{{legend Legendary Action Uses: 3. Immediately after another creature's turn, The Cataclysm's Echo can expend a use to take one of the following actions. The cataclysm's echo regains expended uses at the start of each of its turns.}}

***Rift Lash (Costs 1 Action).*** The Echo makes one Rift Lash attack.

***Rift Step, Short (Costs 1 Action).*** The Echo teleports up to 20 feet.

***Reality Flicker (Costs 2 Actions).*** One creature within 60 feet must reroll the most recent d20 roll it made and use the new result — even fate isn't stable this close to the Echo.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Echo Shard* (2,000 gp): a splinter of unstable reality, usable as a one-use focus for a *wish*-grade ritual or a *teleportation circle*. *Unstable Shell Fragment* (1,000 gp): armor-quality plating that flickers between places. Nothing material remains in its lair, but the objects it swallowed may reappear on the ground nearby, such as a *Staff of Power*, a *Ring of Spell Turning* or a *Rod of Lordly Might*.

**Lore.** Whether it is a creature or the Catastrophe itself, nobody can say. It speaks in a voice that sounds like everyone who was there when the source broke.

**Ecology.** A Gargantuan, ever-changing form of light, shattered glass and distorted air, bigger than a building and impossible to describe the same way twice. It hovers over the deepest scars of the Catastrophe, lingers where the most died, and does not seem to need to eat.

**Initiative**  :: +5 (15)

**Skills**  :: Perception +11
