### Traits
***Living Rift.*** At the start of the Refrain's turn, roll a d8 and apply the corresponding effect in a 40-foot radius centered on it (GM's discretion or a prepared wild-magic surge table). Reality around it does not hold still.

***Echo of Everything.*** Whenever a creature within 120 feet of the Refrain dies, it regains 30 Hit Points.

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the Refrain fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

***Repeated Casting.*** When a creature within 120 feet of the Refrain casts a spell of 1st level or higher, the Refrain can use its Reaction to cast the same spell at the same level, using Charisma as its spellcasting ability (spell save DC 21, +13 to hit with spell attacks) and requiring no components.

***Dead-Magic Withering.*** In a dead-magic zone or an *antimagic field*, the Refrain loses Living Rift, Repeated Casting and The Breaking, and has Disadvantage on attack rolls.

***Last Words.*** As a Bonus Action, a creature within 60 feet of the Refrain can speak aloud the true name of someone who died in the Catastrophe, or something they said at the end. The Refrain makes a DC 21 Charisma saving throw. *Failure:* It can't take Reactions or Legendary Actions until the end of its next turn. *Success:* No effect, and the speaker takes 22 (4d10) Psychic damage.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The Refrain makes four Rift Lash attacks.

***Rift Lash.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +12, reach 20 ft. *Hit:* 28 (4d10 + 6) Force damage, and the target is teleported up to 10 feet in a random direction (GM's choice or roll a d8 for facing).

***Unmaking Pulse (Recharge 5–6).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 20, each creature within 40 feet of the Refrain. *Failure:* 70 (20d6) Force damage. *Success:* Half damage. A creature reduced below half its Hit Point maximum by this damage also suffers a wild-magic surge effect, regardless of the save.

***The Breaking (1/Day).*** A silent flash fills a 120-foot-radius Sphere centered on the Refrain. *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 20, each creature in the area. *Failure:* 66 (12d10) Force damage and the target has the Stunned condition until the end of its next turn. *Success:* Half damage only. For 1 minute afterward, whenever a creature in the area casts a spell, it rolls a d6, and on a 1 a wild-magic surge occurs.

### Bonus Actions
***Rift Step.*** The Refrain teleports up to 120 feet to an unoccupied space it can see.

### Legendary Actions (3/Turn)
{{legend Legendary Action Uses: 3. Immediately after another creature's turn, The Cataclysm's Refrain can expend a use to take one of the following actions. The cataclysm's refrain regains expended uses at the start of each of its turns.}}

***Rift Lash (Costs 1 Action).*** The Refrain makes one Rift Lash attack.

***Rift Step, Short (Costs 1 Action).*** The Refrain teleports up to 40 feet.

***Reality Flicker (Costs 2 Actions).*** One creature within 120 feet must reroll the most recent d20 roll it made and use the new result.

***Replay (Costs 3 Actions).*** The Refrain repeats the last Multiattack it made on its previous turn.

}}



**Loot.** *Refrain Shard* (5,000 gp): a splinter of reality that has happened more than once, usable as the focus for a *wish*-grade ritual or a *plane shift*. *Unstable Shell Plate* (2,500 gp): armor-quality plating that flickers between places and half-remembers shapes it has had. Nothing material remains in its lair, but the objects it swallowed may reappear nearby, including a *Rod of Absorption*, a *Staff of the Magi* or a *Robe of the Archmagi*.

**Lore.** The Echo is the Catastrophe still happening. The Refrain is the Catastrophe happening again, in the same place, with the same people, in the same words. Those who have heard it say it recites the final hour of the day the source broke, one voice at a time, and that if you listen carefully you can hear your own.

**Ecology.** A Gargantuan, slowly turning ring of shattered light, torn air and half-seen faces, as large as a keep and impossible to describe the same way twice. It hangs above the deepest scars of the Catastrophe, but unlike the Echo it appears where the worst surges have recurred. It does not eat or sleep, and it fades in a dead-magic zone.

**Tactics.** It opens with The Breaking when the party is within 120 feet, then stays at the centre of its Living Rift and lashes the nearest creature, copying spells with Repeated Casting so that casters are punished for acting. It heals from deaths, so keep your allies alive. The party's best answers are not damage: a dead-magic zone strips most of its powers, and a character who speaks aloud the name of someone lost in the Catastrophe shuts its Reactions down for a round.


\page

# Living Spells
*Spells that were mid-cast when the source broke, and never ended.*

A living spell is a spell effect that took on sentience and whose energy endures indefinitely. It is a Construct and doesn't require air, food, drink, or sleep. Ten years after the Catastrophe they drift through wild-magic zones and old battlefields, feeding on ambient magic and lashing out at whatever they touch. The compendium covers spells of 1st through 9th level, from a fog cloud that drifts across a farm to a meteor swarm that crosses the horizon.

**Never-finished casting.** A living spell is not made over time but in one moment: a spell cast in the instant the source broke, and never allowed to finish. More have formed since, in surges and at the sites of great casting, because every surge may complete a spell that was never meant to last. They are almost entirely instinct, repeating the effect they were cast to produce on whoever is near. The more complex the spell, the more it seems to have a shape of its own, and the Witch Bolt and the Caustic Brew look a lot like animals.

**Natural counters.** A living spell is still a spell, and the world's ordinary counters work on it: sand for Grease, wind for Fog Cloud, a *silence* spell for Thunderwave, a *shield* for Magic Missile. They are hard to hurt, but easy to outthink.

**How these were built.** Each uses the official Living Spells rules (*Eberron: Rising from the Last War*). Living burning hands (CR 1) is the base for 1st-level spells, with the Damage Immunity, Magical Strike and Spell Mimicry swaps the rules call for. The Magical Strike attack bonus and the Spell Mimicry save DC follow the originals: Proficiency Bonus plus the Constitution modifier. The official rules only cover damage-dealing evocation and conjuration spells, so Fog Cloud, Grease, Sleep and Entangle use a thematic damage type for their immunity and Magical Strike. CRs below 1 are scaled down from living burning hands. CRs 2 and 3 are interpolated between it and the CR 5 living lightning bolt, which is why the CR 3 spells are Large and make two Magical Strikes.

**Higher-level spells.** Spells of 2nd to 9th level scale up from the CR 5 living lightning bolt. Spell Mimicry deals roughly the spell's full damage at its listed level, with Recharge 5–6. Size grows with CR: Large at CR 3 to 6, Huge at CR 7 to 12 and Gargantuan from CR 13. The exceptions are Power Word Kill, which is Tiny, and Time Stop, which is Medium. From CR 10, a living spell gains Legendary Resistance, and from CR 15 it gains Legendary Actions. Spells that don't deal damage have a thematic damage type, as above. The two 9th-level spells that kill or skip a creature's turn, Power Word Kill and Time Stop, recharge on a 6 only. Every higher-level spell also has a natural counter, usually a roleplaying or combat answer: a mirror for Disintegrate, a metal stake for Chain Lightning, a ball of string for Maze, a cantrip to bait Counterspell.

| Living Spell | Level | CR | Spell Mimicry Effect |
|:---|:---:|:---:|:---|
| Fog Cloud | 1 | 1/4 | Heavily Obscured fog |
| Earth Tremor | 1 | 1/4 | Prone, Difficult Terrain |
| Grease | 1 | 1/2 | Slick ground, Prone |
| Thunderwave | 1 | 1 | Thunder blast and push |
| Magic Missile | 1 | 1 | Three unerring darts |
| Ray of Sickness | 1 | 1 | Poison ray, Poisoned |
| Sleep | 1 | 2 | Incapacitated, then Unconscious |
| Ice Knife | 1 | 2 | Shard and frost burst |
| Entangle | 1 | 2 | Restrained, Difficult Terrain |
| Witch Bolt | 1 | 3 | Lightning tether |
| Caustic Brew | 1 | 3 | Clinging acid |
| Misty Step | 2 | 2 | Teleport and thunder burst |
| Web | 2 | 3 | Restraining webbing |
| Scorching Ray | 2 | 3 | Three fire rays |
| Counterspell | 3 | 4 | Cancels a spell as a Reaction |
| Hypnotic Pattern | 3 | 4 | Charmed, Incapacitated |
| Enemies Abound | 3 | 4 | Friend and foe confused |
| Fireball | 3 | 5 | Fire sphere, death explosion |
| Polymorph | 4 | 5 | Turned into a small Beast |
| Ice Storm | 4 | 6 | Hail and cold cylinder |
| Banishment | 4 | 6 | Banished to a demiplane |
| Wall of Force | 5 | 7 | Invisible wall |
| Hold Monster | 5 | 7 | Paralyzed |
| Cone of Cold | 5 | 8 | Cold cone, frozen statues |
| Globe of Invulnerability | 6 | 9 | Spell-proof sphere |
| Chain Lightning | 6 | 10 | Lightning arcs between metal |
| Disintegrate | 6 | 10 | Dust ray |
| Forcecage | 7 | 10 | Cage of force |
| Finger of Death | 7 | 12 | Necrotic ray, Zombies |
| Prismatic Spray | 7 | 12 | Seven coloured rays |
| Antimagic Field | 8 | 11 | Suppresses magic |
| Maze | 8 | 13 | Banished to a labyrinth |
| Sunburst | 8 | 14 | Radiant blast, Blinded |
| Power Word Kill | 9 | 15 | Kills or crushes the mind |
| Time Stop | 9 | 16 | Incapacitates, extra turn |
| Meteor Swarm | 9 | 17 | Four delayed meteors |

**Family rules.**
- **Amorphous.** They can squeeze through a gap as narrow as an inch.
- **Magic Resistance.** They have Advantage on saving throws against spells and magical effects.
- **Spell Mimicry.** Each repeats its original spell, with a recharge.
- **Unique quirk.** Each has a natural counter, as above.
- **Legendary Resistance.** From CR 10, three times per day, a living spell that fails a save can succeed instead.

**Lore.** Fishing villages say it forms from a fog bank someone conjured in the instant the source broke, and that it drifts toward anyone who is lost.

**Ecology.** It follows rivers and coastlines, thins out in dry air, and never quite vanishes. Alone it is a nuisance; smugglers and ambushers like to follow one.

**Tactics.** It opens with Spell Mimicry and fights from inside the fog, never pursuing past its edge. A strong wind clears the fog and leaves a very small, very ordinary threat.

**Immunities**  :: Bludgeoning; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 9
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 1/4 (XP 50; PB +2)

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

### Actions
***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +3, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 4 (1d6 + 1) Bludgeoning damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 11, each creature in a 10-foot Emanation originating from the living spell. *Failure:* 3 (1d6) Bludgeoning damage and the target has the Prone condition. *Success:* No damage or condition. Loose earth or stone in the area becomes Difficult Terrain until the end of the living spell's next turn.

}}

**Loot.** *Shiver-Stone* (5 gp): a pebble that trembles near unstable ground, sold to miners as a cheap cave-in warning.

**Lore.** Delvers swear it is the aftershock of the first quake, still looking for the wall it was meant to bring down.

**Ecology.** It haunts mines, cellars and rubble fields, drifting just above the ground and grumbling against it. It can't bring down solid structures, only shake what is already loose.

**Tactics.** It darts in, triggers Spell Mimicry to knock a group prone, then hits fallen targets with Magical Strike. A creature standing on a raised surface, such as a wagon bed, table or rope bridge, can reasonably be given Advantage on the save.

A creature that enters the area or ends its turn there must also succeed on this save or have the Prone condition.

}}

**Loot.** *Sheen Residue* (5 gp): a waxy film scraped from where it fell. Smeared on one door hinge or boot sole, it works once and then dries out.

**Lore.** Cooks and stable hands tell stories of a puddle that follows you from room to room. Everyone who has met one agrees it is smug about it.

**Ecology.** It oozes along floors and cobbles and avoids dry, gritty ground. It is happiest in kitchens, tanneries and ruined inns.

**Tactics.** It lays its slick patch across a doorway or narrow path, then attacks whoever is sprawled on the other side. Sand or ash tipped onto the grease ends the effect for that 5-foot square.

**Loot.** *Thunderclap Seed* (15 gp): a brittle husk that gives off a muffled boom, audible out to 300 feet, when crushed. It is a one-use alarm.

**Lore.** Soldiers who survived the first days report a wall of sound that arrived with no caster behind it. They called it an echo of the spell, but it is the spell itself.

**Ecology.** It rides canyon winds and favors places that carry sound well, such as gorges, bell towers and empty halls. It goes quiet and sluggish in thick snow and deep forest.

**Tactics.** It announces itself, since the blast carries 300 feet, and shoves a group off a ledge or into one another with Spell Mimicry. A *silence* spell stops it hitting anything inside the sphere, because creatures there are immune to thunder damage.

**Loot.** *Dartglass Splinters* (25 gp for a set of three): slivers of hardened force. Loaded in a sling or crossbow, each counts as magical for overcoming resistance, once. A scribe can also copy its remnant onto parchment to make a *Spell Scroll* (*magic missile*) with a DC 13 Arcana check.

**Lore.** In the first hour of the Catastrophe a thousand apprentices cast this spell at once, and it seems a few of those casts never found a target.

**Ecology.** It circles old duelling grounds, wizard towers and battlefields, and idles in quiet places by drifting in slow loops. It dims when kept from magic for a long time but doesn't fade.

**Tactics.** It stays out of reach and uses Spell Mimicry on the weakest-looking creature in range, since the darts can't miss. A *shield* spell cast in response negates the darts, as it does for the spell it mimics.

**Loot.** *Green Residue* (10 gp): a sickly-green smear that works as a base for a weak poison and counts as a poisoner's component for a single dose.

**Lore.** Healers in plague country still tell of a green beam that turns up where no one is casting, and of the survivors who found it hours later, still drifting.

**Ecology.** It clings to marshes, fever-wards and abandoned hospitals. Cold air slows it, and it never goes near open flames.

**Tactics.** It stays at range and strikes at whoever carries the most visible healing supplies. Any cover that blocks line of sight, including Total Cover, stops the ray.

On a second failure, the target has the Unconscious condition for 1 minute (until the living spell's concentration ends, as if concentrating on a spell). }}

**Loot.** *Lullaby Dust* (30 gp): a pinch of faintly glittering dust. Sprinkled on a pillow, it guarantees one night of uninterrupted rest and no dreams.

**Lore.** Whole camps woke after the Catastrophe with no memory of the night before and no sign of why. Healers blame this thing, which drifts silently and only strikes after the first yawn.

**Ecology.** It feeds on the calm of sleeping creatures and slides easily through shutters and keyholes. It is drawn to dark, quiet places such as inns, barracks and bedrooms.

**Tactics.** It targets a clustered group, and since its sphere is only 5 feet wide it favors a doorway or tight camp. Any damage wakes a sleeper, so an ally who stays awake to splash water or deal a light blow can break the effect early.
\page

**Loot.** *Frost Shard* (25 gp): a palm-length sliver of ice that stays frozen for a tenday. It's prized as a ritual dagger, and is also one of the few things that keeps a corpse from spoiling.

**Lore.** It first appeared at the high, cold passes where hunters were caught mid-cast by the surge. It remembers one thing, which is to hit and then shatter.

**Ecology.** It hangs in cold air and mountain shade, and weakens in warmth. In summer it sinks to cellars and caves.

**Tactics.** It aims its shard at a group's center and relies on the frost burst to catch anyone standing close. Fire damage doesn't hurt it any more than normal, but standing in direct warmth, such as beside a bonfire, makes it flicker and hesitate.

**Loot.** *Arc-Wire* (50 gp): a coil of dead lightning that stays warm and works as a one-use tether, making a creature it's wrapped around unable to move more than 30 feet from a chosen point for 1 minute. It can also stand in for the component of a *lightning bolt* scroll.

**Lore.** Survivors who were tethered describe the same thing: a hum like a held note, and a slow understanding that it could keep this up as long as it liked.

**Ecology.** It clings to storm fronts and dead trees, and will happily follow a thunderstorm for days. It is attracted to anything metallic.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry on a target it can pin, such as a fleeing caster, and alternates Tether Surge with its Multiattack once others come close. Breaking line of sight or getting more than 30 feet away ends the tether, so a smart party splits it up rather than standing and fighting.

**Loot.** *Etching Brine* (40 gp): a bottle of its residue, kept sealed, which etches a deep mark into metal or stone and can be used to cut a lock or runic inscription. It eats through most containers within a day.

**Lore.** The alchemists' quarter in old cities was the worst hit. What drifts out of the ruined cellars now is a spill that never finished spilling.

**Ecology.** It slides along gutters and cellar floors, leaving etched channels in the stone, and dissolves any ordinary vessel it is trapped in. It is drawn to the smell of spoiled food and rotten wood.

**Tactics.** It fires the Line across a group, then makes its Multiattack on whoever is covered in acid. Water, or a quick scrape from a friend, removes the coating, so a party that spends an action rescuing an ally is trading tempo for safety.
\page

**Loot.** *Weed-Whip* (20 gp): a length of living vine that tightens on command once, as a one-use rope that binds a creature of Medium size or smaller (escape DC 12). *Binding Sap* (30 gp): sticky, quick-drying, a decent adhesive. It leaves no other remains.

**Lore.** Farmers and foresters tell of the day the grass stood up and held the field. Nobody was hurt, they say, but nobody moved for an hour.

**Ecology.** A shifting patch of tangled grass, reed and vine, drifting a little above the ground, with ropy tendrils that coil and uncoil as it hunts. It lingers in meadows, hedgerows and overgrown ruins, keeps low, and thrives in damp places. It cannot cross bare stone or ash for long and burns readily.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry first, to hold as many targets as it can, then attacks the nearest one. It avoids fire and doesn't pursue fleeing creatures beyond the area it has taken. A good cut with a blade frees a restrained creature quickly, and fire ends the effect in that square.

**Loot.** *Silver Mist Vial* (35 gp): a small bottle that holds a pinch of silvery vapour. It can be opened once, wrapping the user in mist that grants +2 to their next Stealth check and any single teleportation they cast in the next minute lands 5 feet further. *Echo Shell* (20 gp): a conch that repeats the last thing it heard in a quiet voice, once.

**Lore.** Gnomes of the old Quillhaven Guild say they have seen it a dozen times, and the only way to tell one is the one leaving. They also say a few of them are the same one.

**Ecology.** A silver shimmer shaped like a shallow arc, like a heat haze that remembers being a person. It blinks constantly from place to place with a soft clap of air. It likes dim places and avoids sunlight on bare metal.

**Tactics.** It prefers to fight from a distance, popping in behind casters and leaving a clap of thunder in its wake. Cold iron hurts it, and while a creature within 5 feet of it holds or wears cold iron, it can't teleport at all. Bring an iron nail or a horseshoe and the creature has to fight on foot.

|Int|3|--4|--4|Wis|10|+0|+0|Cha|6|--2|--2|

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Vulnerabilities**  :: Fire
**Immunities**  :: Poison; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 10
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 3 (XP 700; PB +2)

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 11 (2d6 + 4) Poison damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 14, each creature in a 20-foot Cube centered on a point the living spell can see within 60 feet. *Failure:* The target has the Restrained condition from sticky webbing (escape DC 14; the webbing also gives way to 5 damage from a slashing or fire source). The Cube is Difficult Terrain for 1 minute or until the living spell's concentration ends (as if concentrating on a spell). A 5-foot square of the web that is exposed to fire burns away at the end of the round, dealing 5 (2d4) Fire damage to any creature in it. *Success:* No effect.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Silk-Cord* (30 gp): a hank of tough, nearly weightless web that does not stick unless wet. It works as 50 feet of silk rope, and it is far lighter than ordinary rope. *Venom Residue* (25 gp): a tacky, faintly sweet paste that a poisoner can use as a base for a sleep poison. It leaves no other remains.

**Lore.** A spiderweb is a patient thing. These are what a web becomes when it has nothing left to be patient for. People who found one in a cellar describe walls that were ordinary a minute before, then threads from every direction.

**Ecology.** A drifting, sagging tangle of grey threads that hangs a few feet above the ground like a cloud of caught breath. It strands itself across doorways, stairwells and alleys, and mostly it waits. It is drawn to the warmth of living things, and it has never been seen to eat.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry as soon as there are two or more targets in a space it can cover, then attacks the Restrained ones with its Multiattack. A flaming torch, a *fire bolt* or any other fire shreds the Cube quickly, and it is Vulnerable to Fire. A party that has anything to light will fare well. A party that doesn't should kill it from outside the Cube.
\page

### Traits

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

### Actions

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 11 (2d6 + 4) Fire damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** The living spell makes three Scorching Ray attacks, each at a different target or all at one. *Ranged Attack Roll:* +6, range 120 ft. *Hit:* 7 (2d6) Fire damage. A flammable object that isn't worn or carried ignites if hit.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Cinder-Heart* (45 gp): a glowing coal that stays warm for a year and works as a pocket warmer or a one-use fire starter. *Ray-Lens* (60 gp): a shard of glass fused by a ray, which, set into a lantern, throws light and heat in a narrow beam to 60 feet. A caster who studies it can use it as the material component for *scorching ray* in place of the usual one.

**Lore.** It was one of the last things cast on the day of the Catastrophe, a ray meant to be thrown three times, and it was thrown for ever. The spell now keeps throwing, and nobody is quite sure when it is going to stop.

**Ecology.** A ball of red-orange heat that sits low in the air with three lines of glowing light radiating from it. Each one twists toward a target on its own. It is drawn to open flames and to cold, damp places where its heat can steam.

**Tactics.** It opens with Spell Mimicry, firing one ray each at three different targets to force three different decisions, and follows up with Multiattack on whoever steps close. Cold damage staggers it, and it is Vulnerable to Cold. A *ray of frost* or a bucket of ice water makes a dramatic difference.
\page

## Living Counterspell
*Large Construct, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 15 (natural armor)
**HP**  :: 66 (7d10 + 28)
**Speed**  :: 25 ft., Fly 25 ft. (hover)

**Initiative**  :: +1 (11)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|10|+0|+0|Dex|12|+1|+1|Con|18|+4|+4|
|Int|3|--4|--4|Wis|10|+0|+0|Cha|6|--2|--2|

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Immunities**  :: Force; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 10
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 4 (XP 1,100; PB +2)

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Compulsive Counter.*** The first time on each round that a creature within 60 feet of the living spell casts a spell, the living spell uses Spell Mimicry on it if the ability is available, whether or not the spell is harmful.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 13 (2d8 + 4) Force damage.

### Reactions
***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** *Trigger:* A creature the living spell can see within 60 feet casts a spell. *Response:* The caster makes a Constitution saving throw (DC 14). *Failure:* The spell dissipates with no effect, and the action, Bonus Action or Reaction used to cast it is wasted. If the spell was cast with a spell slot, the slot isn't expended. *Success:* The spell takes effect normally.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Hushed Rune* (80 gp): a palm-sized slate etched with a silence rune. Pressed to a spellbook it protects it from *detect magic* for 24 hours. *Null Thread* (60 gp): a strand that, tied around a wrist, grants Advantage on the next saving throw against a spell of 3rd level or lower. It leaves only one of these, and only the first time.

**Lore.** It was cast at the very end by a mage who knew, or hoped, that the thing that was happening could be stopped. It didn't stop it, but the counterspell is still looking for the thing it is supposed to stop.

**Ecology.** A humming sphere of cool grey light with a blank mask-like surface that faces whoever last spoke. It follows gatherings of spellcasters, such as schools and guild halls, at a polite distance. Unlike the others it makes no sound at all.

**Tactics.** It counters the first spell cast near it each round, even a harmless one, and then has nothing left until its Reaction recharges. A party that casts something cheap first, such as *light* or *mage hand*, burns its Reaction and leaves the spell that matters free. It must see the caster, so Total Cover or a *darkness* spell between them also stops it.
\page

***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 13 (2d8 + 4) Psychic damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 14, each creature in a 30-foot Cube centered on a point the living spell can see within 120 feet that can see the pattern. *Failure:* The target has the Charmed and Incapacitated conditions, and its Speed is 0, for 1 minute or until the living spell's concentration ends (as if concentrating on a spell). \column

**Loot.** *Swirl-Glass* (70 gp): a colourful glass lens that, held up, makes swirls of colour dance and lets its holder make a Charisma (Performance or Deception) check with Advantage once. *Dream-Dust* (40 gp): a pinch of fine multicoloured powder that adds a bright shimmer to light.

**Lore.** At a festival, people said, someone wove a pattern of light into the sky to delight a crowd. Nobody who looked at it ever remembered what the festival was for.

**Ecology.** A drifting spiral of coloured light, many-hued and slow, rippling like silk. It prefers a clear view in all directions. Guild halls, lanterns and festival grounds attract it, and it seems to like being watched.

**Tactics.** It opens by stopping as many creatures as it can with Spell Mimicry, then attacks someone who isn't. Averting your eyes from the pattern, a blindfold or a *darkness* spell protects against the effect, though it also makes it hard to hit the living spell. Anyone caught in it can be shaken out by an ally, so a party that keeps its wits can usually win.

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Immunities**  :: Psychic; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 10

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Familiar Voice.*** A creature within 5 feet of an affected target can use an action to speak its name and who it is, making a DC 14 Wisdom (Insight) check. On a success, the target repeats the save with Advantage.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +6, reach 10 ft. *Hit:* 13 (2d8 + 4) Psychic damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** *Intelligence Saving Throw:* DC 14, one creature the living spell can see within 120 feet. *Failure:* For 1 minute or until the living spell's concentration ends (as if concentrating on a spell), the target can't tell friend from foe: it regards every creature it can see as an enemy. Whenever it takes damage, it repeats the save, ending the effect on itself on a success. On each of its turns, the target must take the Attack action (or cast an attack spell) against the nearest creature other than itself, and has Disadvantage on other actions it tries to take. If no other creature is within reach, it moves toward the nearest one. *Success:* No effect. A target that is Blinded or can't see is unaffected.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Muddle-Glass* (90 gp): a smoky lens that, held to the eye, makes everyone look like a stranger, which grants Advantage on one Deception check made to hide in a crowd. *Discord Seed* (50 gp): a dry brown pod that rattles when two creatures nearby are hostile to each other, and is useful to an interrogator or a diplomat. It leaves no other remains.

**Lore.** A general cast it on the opposing army at the very moment of the Catastrophe. Both armies were wearing the same colours by then, and nobody on the field could say which side had cast it.

**Ecology.** A sullen, drifting cloud of smoky grey-violet haze, filled with the faint shapes of faces that look alike and hostile. It follows crowds, camps and marketplaces, where it can set people on each other. It is quiet, and it seems patient.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry on the best-armed fighter, so that the party's strongest attacker turns on its own friends, then stands back and uses Multiattack on whoever is left. Damage lets the victim repeat the save, so a gentle tap from an ally can break the effect, though it will be hard to deliver it with the victim swinging. A blindfold or darkness stops the spell from working, since the target needs to see its enemies. Calling the victim by name and saying who you are, on a DC 14 Wisdom (Insight) check as an action, also lets it repeat the save with Advantage.
\page

\column

**Loot.** *Bead of Slow Fire* (150 gp): a glowing glass bead that, if thrown, explodes in a 10-foot radius for 14 (4d6) Fire damage (Dexterity save DC 13 for half). It is usable once. *Ashen Heart* (100 gp): the dull core it leaves behind, which can serve as the material component for casting *fireball* in place of the bat guano and sulphur. Rarely, a single bead of a *Necklace of Fireballs* is fused into its core.

**Lore.** The oldest accounts, of the first cast on the day of the Catastrophe, describe a bead of light the size of a pea, thrown too late to explode, that floated past everyone for three days. It is believed that it got larger every time.

**Ecology.** A boiling ball of orange flame, roughly 10 feet across with a visible dark spot in the middle where the bead sits. It keeps to open ground such as plazas, battlefields and fields and dislikes narrow spaces. It flies only a little above the ground, leaving scorch marks.

**Tactics.** It opens with Spell Mimicry on the largest cluster of targets, then fights with Multiattack on the nearest enemy. When it is killed it explodes, so a party that stays at range after a lucky critical hit will fare better than one that closes in. Killing it with Cold damage prevents the explosion, and Cold also hurts it badly.

\column

**Loot.** *Shapeshifter's Tooth* (75 gp): a small fang that, when carried, gives Advantage on the first Wisdom saving throw you make against a transmutation effect each day. *Chimera Hair* (40 gp): a strand that takes on the colour of its surroundings; a tailor will pay for it.

**Lore.** A mage cast it on herself in the last second to become something that could survive. It worked for as long as the spell lasted, and the spell has been lasting ever since.

**Ecology.** A swirling, shifting form that never settles on a shape: a stag one moment, a tangle of vines the next, a rabbit the one after that. It prefers forest edges and old menageries. It is calm, and a little sad.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry on the most dangerous melee creature, then fights through its Multiattack. A transformed creature has its mind intact and is not helpless, so a party should treat the transformed ally as if they were a different character. Speaking the creature's name to it and succeeding on a Wisdom (Insight) check snaps it back, which makes this one a roleplaying encounter as much as a fight.

**Loot.** *Hailstone Core* (120 gp): a fist-sized sphere of clear ice that never melts. It chills a pitcher of drink instantly, and as a thrown weapon it deals 3 (1d6) Bludgeoning plus 3 (1d6) Cold damage. *Frost-Shards* (45 gp): a pouch of shards, useful as a component for *ice knife* and *cone of cold*.

**Lore.** In the first winter after the Catastrophe, a storm broke over the eastern valleys in the middle of high summer. It rained hail for six days, and then, quietly, it stopped raining and started to wander.

**Ecology.** A heavy, ragged cloud of grey cloud-stuff, trailing curtains of ice that drift beneath it. It follows high ground, eastward winds and the fringes of rifts. The ground beneath it is crusted with white.

**Tactics.** It opens with Spell Mimicry on the biggest group it can see, then slams whoever comes close with Multiattack. Fire greatly weakens it, because it is Vulnerable to Fire, and open flame causes it to sputter. A good *fire bolt* or two will do more than a blade.

### Actions

\column

**Loot.** *Doorless Key* (200 gp): a sliver of grey metal that vibrates faintly near the edges of a planar boundary. *Hollow Cord* (90 gp): a thin length of rope that seems to lead somewhere else; one end can be pulled free, and the user will find the other end a hundred feet away. It fetches a good price from smugglers.

**Lore.** It is one of the few that had a purpose, say the Gatewardens of the old college. Someone wanted another person to be anywhere else, and the spell has been working on the idea for ten years.

**Ecology.** A pale ring in the air, like a frame without a picture, through which the world looks slightly different. It lingers near rifts, planar scars and the sites of old duels. It is calm, and strangely quiet.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry on the biggest threat it can see and then fights the rest with Multiattack. While it holds someone, it is easy to damage it: every time it takes damage, it makes a Constitution save with Disadvantage or loses concentration. Ending the effect brings the target back at once, and a well-timed hit saves a friend.

|Str|12|+1|+1|Dex|10|+0|+0|Con|20|+5|+8|
|Int|3|--4|--4|Wis|12|+1|+4|Cha|6|--2|--2|

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Immunities**  :: Force; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 11
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 7 (XP 2,900; PB +3)

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Brittle Core.*** If the living spell takes 25 or more damage in a single turn, it loses concentration on Spell Mimicry.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +8, reach 15 ft. *Hit:* 23 (4d8 + 5) Force damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** The living spell raises an invisible wall of force at a point it can see within 60 feet, either a flat panel up to 30 feet long, 10 feet high and 1 inch thick, or a Sphere or Hemisphere with a 10-foot radius. Creatures in the wall's space are moved to the nearest unoccupied space on one side of it, as the living spell chooses. Nothing can physically pass through the wall, and it can't be damaged by any source. It lasts for 1 minute or until the living spell's concentration ends (as if concentrating on a spell). The wall also ends if the living spell is more than 60 feet from it. A *disintegrate* spell destroys it instantly.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Panel of Clear Force* (400 gp): a hand-sized, nearly invisible glass pane that cannot be broken. It lasts as a one-use shield, granting +2 AC until the end of your next turn, or a window in a place no window should be. *Force-Grain* (90 gp): fine dust that keeps its shape under pressure and makes a very fine abrasive.

**Lore.** Nobody who builds walls knows of this one. It was cast in the last moments by a mage who wanted a wall between two things. The two things are long gone, but the wall is not.

**Ecology.** An invisible presence that is only visible as a faint shimmer in the air, like heat shimmer without the heat. Its core is a pale blue lens the size of a coffin lid. It moves slowly, prefers corridors and bridges, and takes strange pleasure in making people turn back.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry to divide the party, trapping the most dangerous melee fighter on one side and the casters on the other, or to wall itself in as a defence. Fight the core and not the wall. A single hit of 25 or more damage in one turn shatters its concentration, so a big smite or a critical *eldritch blast* ends the barrier at once.
\page

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +8, reach 15 ft. *Hit:* 23 (4d8 + 5) Necrotic damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 16, one creature the living spell can see within 90 feet. *Failure:* The target has the Paralyzed condition for 1 minute or until the living spell's concentration ends (as if concentrating on a spell). At the end of each of its turns, the target repeats the save, ending the effect on itself on a success. The effect ends early if the target is more than 90 feet from the living spell or out of its line of sight. *Success:* No effect.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Stillness Salt* (180 gp): a pinch of grey salt that, when thrown into a creature's face, causes the Restrained condition for 1 round (DC 15 Constitution save negates). *Held-Breath Phial* (120 gp): a little bottle that smells of nothing, and holds breath for ten minutes of underwater breathing.

**Lore.** A soldier who survived one said that his body didn't hurt at all. It simply stopped, all of it, while the world went on moving around him and he watched.

**Ecology.** A pale, silent haze with a hundred faint fingers stretched in front of it. Whatever lies in front of it seems more still than the stillness around it. It haunts battlefields and dungeon corridors, where there are many things that cannot move.

**Tactics.** It picks the most dangerous opponent with Spell Mimicry and then keeps its Multiattack for those who try to rescue them. A held creature is still aware and can see what's happening, so teams can plan to break the line of sight or the 90-foot range. A *freedom of movement* spell grants immunity.

**Initiative**  :: +0 (10)

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Vulnerabilities**  :: Fire
**Immunities**  :: Cold; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 11
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 8 (XP 3,900; PB +3)

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +8, reach 15 ft. *Hit:* 27 (4d10 + 5) Cold damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 16, each creature in a 60-foot Cone originating from the living spell. *Failure:* 36 (8d8) Cold damage. *Success:* Half damage. A creature reduced to 0 Hit Points by this damage is turned to a frozen statue until it thaws, which takes 1 hour of warmth.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Heart of Winter* (400 gp): a fist-sized lump of blue ice that stays frozen in any heat. Held against a creature it deals 7 (2d6) Cold damage once per day, and it chills a hall for days. *Rime-Hide* (150 gp): a frosted sheet that can be tailored into a cloak granting Resistance to Cold damage for 1 hour, after which it must be left in snow to recover.

**Lore.** Winter came to a thousand villages in the first week and left, they say, with a hundred frozen folk standing in the doorways. It is said that thawing them out is the only mercy, and that the survivors remember nothing.

**Ecology.** A sweeping white-blue wedge of ice-laden wind with a vague, crystalline heart. Frosted air drifts outward from it in a cone, and wherever it has passed the ground is hard and still. It keeps to cold ground and high places, and melts a little in strong sun.

**Tactics.** It opens by turning toward the largest group and unleashing Spell Mimicry in a sweeping cone, then follows up with Multiattack on whoever remains. Fire is its great weakness, and a single *burning hands* or a lit torch can undo much of its work. Frozen allies can be thawed with warmth in an hour, so a lost battle need not be a lost friend.
\page

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Hollow Core.*** A spell of 6th level or higher, a non-spell magical effect (such as an item's ability) or a weapon attack ignores the Sphere from Spell Mimicry.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +9, reach 15 ft. *Hit:* 32 (5d10 + 5) Force damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** The living spell surrounds itself with a shimmering 10-foot-radius Sphere for 1 minute or until its concentration ends (as if concentrating on a spell). Spells of level 5 or lower cast from outside the Sphere can't affect the living spell or any creature or object within it, even if cast with a higher-level slot, and such spells cast from inside can't affect anything outside. When the Sphere forms, it sends out a burst. *Strength Saving Throw:* DC 17, each creature within 10 feet of the living spell. *Failure:* 22 (4d10) Force damage and the creature is pushed 10 feet away. *Success:* Half damage and no push.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Globe-Glass* (500 gp): a perfect sphere of clear glass that gives off a faint silvery shimmer and, once, grants a creature holding it Advantage on saving throws against spells of 4th level or lower for 1 minute. *Shell of Stillness* (250 gp): a shard that can be set into a *ring of spell storing* for no extra cost.

**Lore.** Old accounts say that the spell sealed a mage into a space where spells could not reach, and that she survived. They don't say what became of the mage.

**Ecology.** A thick, silvery sphere of faintly glowing glass-like energy, 10 feet across, with a dim shape at the centre. It rolls and drifts, never touching the ground. It prefers wizards' ruins, and sits for days in a closed circle, unseen.

**Tactics.** Casters should save their best spells for a spell of level 6 or higher, or for effects that aren't spells at all, such as swords, arrows and alchemist's fire. It fights with Multiattack and uses its globe when casters outnumber melee fighters. The Sphere itself is only magic, and a *dispel magic* cast at 6th level ends it.

**Immunities**  :: Lightning; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the living spell fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +10, reach 15 ft. *Hit:* 33 (5d10 + 6) Lightning damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** The living spell fires a bolt at one target it can see within 150 feet, which then arcs to up to three other targets within 30 feet of the first. *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 18, each target. *Failure:* 45 (10d8) Lightning damage. *Success:* Half damage. The first target and each arc must be a creature wearing or carrying metal if one is available. An unattended metal object of at least 5 pounds within range counts as such a target, takes no damage, and ends the chain if the bolt strikes it.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Lightning-Rod Quill* (300 gp): a metal quill that, if planted in the ground, attracts the next lightning strike within 30 feet to itself and fuses harmlessly. *Spark-Gland* (180 gp): a dim, sparking gland that can recharge a *wand of lightning bolts* (rarely) or serve as the material component for *lightning bolt* and *chain lightning*.

**Lore.** Grandmother Voss of the old Pylon Guild remembered the day it formed. It struck a line of her own colleagues, one after another, each passing the bolt on as it had been passed to them.

**Ecology.** A huge, forking crackle of white-blue light in the shape of a ring of arcs, joining in the centre. It drifts across open country and cities, drawn to things made of metal: bridges, towers and rails. It leaves a fine scattering of fused sand wherever it hovers.

**Tactics.** It opens by aiming Spell Mimicry at the party's most heavily armoured fighter and then lets the bolt jump from one metal-wearer to the next. It can be fooled: a metal stake, shield or similar object planted at a distance counts as a target, and the first arc is wasted on it. Take off your helmet and drop your metal weapon, or plant spikes, and you reduce its damage greatly.
\page

\column

**Loot.** *Gray Dust* (350 gp): a pouch of the powdered remains of its ray, which can be used as the material component for *disintegrate*, or for dissolving an ordinary object into nothing, once. *Green Filament* (220 gp): a thin thread of green light that cuts through stone and iron as a fine saw, but will burn away after 1 hour.

**Lore.** Nobody in the old Tower of Three Roads survived to describe how it was cast. The ray was fired at the wall, the floor and the ceiling, and the tower isn't there any more.

**Ecology.** A thin green line of force at the centre of a pale sphere, wavering as it points at things. It prefers dead, empty places where nothing is left to take, such as dry plains, old towers and the lips of rifts. Where it has passed, pits and clean holes are left in walls.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry on the target least likely to dodge it, such as a tank in plate. A polished mirror or shield can send the ray back: a creature holding one can use its Reaction to redirect the ray at another creature within 30 feet of it. After using it, it falls back on Multiattack and hovers out of reach until the ray returns.

***Bound to the Cage.*** If the living spell is reduced to 0 Hit Points, any cage it created disappears.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Cage-Bar* (350 gp): a thick rod of hardened translucent force that never rusts and can be broken only by a *disintegrate* spell. It is excellent as a crossbar for a vault door. *Warden's Key* (180 gp): a small, glassy key that opens any lock made of force, once.

**Lore.** Jailers say the first cage was meant to hold a single dangerous prisoner. The prisoner got out, and the cage went looking for another.

**Ecology.** A cube of faint, glassy bars, roughly 20 feet on a side, floating a hand's width above the ground. It looks like a cage with no prisoner. It drifts along old prison roads, castle courtyards and zoological gardens, patiently and politely.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry on a group of creatures standing together, then picks off the trapped ones one at a time with Multiattack. A caster can teleport out with a successful Charisma saving throw, and Tiny creatures such as a familiar or a sprite can slip through the bars and carry a key, a spell scroll or a message to the others. It dissolves if the core is destroyed.

**Loot.** *Pointing-Bone* (500 gp): a long, finger-shaped bone that glows faintly. A necromancer can use it as the material component for *finger of death*. *Grave-Wax* (260 gp): a candle that burns black and keeps the air cold, and which, lit in a room with a corpse, reveals how it died. *Death Mask* (200 gp): a pale, flat mask that, worn, imposes Disadvantage on *speak with dead* attempts by anyone who looks at it.

**Lore.** A priestess of the old Mourning Order said the living spell does not feel malice. It points at someone, and then it is done with them, and they get up afterward as though nothing has happened.

**Ecology.** A long, pale finger of dark light attached to a thin hand of black smoke, trailing a robe of mist. It drifts through graveyards, battlefields and abandoned hospices. The dead around it are always the quietest in the area.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry on a creature whose death would hurt the party most, then fights with its Multiattack while its new Zombie holds the line. Radiant damage harms it greatly, since it is Vulnerable to Radiant, and a hallowed place stops its Zombies from rising. Kill it and the Zombies collapse at once.

***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the living spell fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

***Needs Light.*** While the living spell is in an area of magical darkness, damage dealt by Spell Mimicry is halved and Spell Mimicry doesn't recharge.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +10, reach 15 ft. *Hit:* 39 (6d10 + 6) Radiant damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** The living spell rolls 1d8 for each creature in a 60-foot Cone originating from it to see which colour strikes that creature. *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 18, each creature in the Cone. *Failure:* The full effect of the ray. *Success:* Half damage from the ray, and none of its other effects. **1—Red.** 35 (10d6) Fire damage. **2—Orange.** 35 (10d6) Acid damage. **3—Yellow.** 35 (10d6) Lightning damage. **4—Green.** 35 (10d6) Poison damage. **5—Blue.** 35 (10d6) Cold damage. **6—Indigo.** The target has the Restrained condition and makes a DC 18 Constitution saving throw at the end of each of its turns. On a success, the Blinded condition ends. **8—Special.** The target is struck by two rays. Roll twice, rerolling any 8.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Spectrum Shards* (320 gp for a set of seven): thumb-sized crystals that each hold a colour of light. Used as a component, they allow a caster to cast *prismatic spray* once. *Rainbow Glass* (140 gp): a prism that scatters light into a bright, harmless rainbow, and a favourite among street performers.

**Lore.** Painters and glass-workers called the day of the Catastrophe the Day of Seven Colours. It was cast over a city's marketplace and cut it into stripes. People are still finding the colours.

**Ecology.** A shifting fan of light, with bands of red, orange, yellow, green, blue, indigo and violet all intertwined. It is lovely to look at, and that is the problem. It follows sunlight and dislikes the dark, and wanders widely at midday.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry on the largest group and then falls back on Multiattack. It needs light to work. In magical darkness its ray damage is halved and Spell Mimicry doesn't recharge, so a *darkness* spell or a heavy cloak thrown over it turns the encounter around.
\page

*Huge Construct, Unaligned*

**AC**  :: 17 (natural armor)
**HP**  :: 212 (17d12 + 102)
**Speed**  :: Fly 40 ft. (hover)

**Initiative**  :: +1 (11)

|   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |   |   | MOD | SAVE |
|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|:--|:-:|:---:|:----:|
|Str|12|+1|+1|Dex|12|+1|+5|Con|22|+6|+10|
|Int|3|--4|--4|Wis|12|+1|+5|Cha|6|--2|--2|

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Immunities**  :: Force; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 120 ft., Passive Perception 11
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 11 (XP 7,200; PB +4)

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the living spell fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

***Cracked Field.*** If the living spell takes Thunder damage, spells and magic items work normally within its Sphere until the end of its next turn.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +10, reach 15 ft. *Hit:* 39 (6d10 + 6) Force damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** The living spell projects a 10-foot-radius Sphere of antimagic around itself for 1 minute or until its concentration ends (as if concentrating on a spell). Within the Sphere, spells and other magical effects are suppressed, magic items function as ordinary items, and creatures summoned by magic vanish until the Sphere ends. Ongoing effects resume if they are still active when the Sphere ends. The living spell, and any effect it creates, is not affected by the Sphere. Magic Resistance and Magic Strike still function normally.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Dead-Iron Wafer* (300 gp): a dull grey disc that suppresses the magic of any item it touches for 1 hour. *Hollow Salt* (120 gp): a pinch that, when sprinkled in a circle, makes a 5-foot circle in which no spell above 2nd level works for 1 minute. The living spell itself leaves almost nothing behind.

**Lore.** Casters whisper that it shouldn't exist: a spell that stops spells, with nothing to stop. It is thought that when the Catastrophe came, a dead zone appeared, and a living spell grew around it.

**Ecology.** A pocket of dull, matte grey where light seems thinner, a smooth round shape that casts no glow. Magic items near it grow dim, and nearby spells fizzle. It wanders slowly through old wizard colleges and battlefields where rifts have closed.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry when casters are within 10 feet and fights with Multiattack from within the field. Step outside the field and it is a plain monster with a reach of 15 feet. Thunder damage cracks the field: if the living spell takes Thunder damage, magic works in the Sphere until the end of its next turn. A caster with a *thunderwave* in reserve is more valuable than a fighter in plate.

|Int|3|--4|--4|Wis|12|+1|+6|Cha|6|--2|--2|

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Immunities**  :: Psychic; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 120 ft., Passive Perception 11

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the living spell fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

***Thread of Ariadne.*** A creature in the demiplane who holds the end of 50 feet or more of thread, string or cord, the other end of which is held by a creature outside it, has Advantage on checks to escape.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +11, reach 20 ft. *Hit:* 39 (6d10 + 6) Psychic damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** *Intelligence Saving Throw:* DC 19, one creature the living spell can see within 60 feet. *Failure:* The target is banished to a labyrinthine demiplane for 10 minutes or until the living spell's concentration ends (as if concentrating on a spell). While there, the target has the Incapacitated condition, and at the end of each of its turns it can make a DC 20 Intelligence (Investigation) check to escape. On a success, it returns to the space it left, or the nearest unoccupied space. A creature holding a ball of string, thread or cord of at least 50 feet, the other end of which is held by a creature outside the demiplane, has Advantage on the check. *Success:* No effect.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Red Thread* (250 gp): a ball of crimson thread that never runs out. It can be used for a *Maze* spell's Investigation check, and it can mark a trail that only the user can see. *Minotaur's Horn* (400 gp): a small curved horn from a creature that guards a labyrinth. It adds +2 to an Intelligence (Investigation) check made in a maze once.

**Lore.** A scholar of mazes said the first maze did not have a minotaur. It had a person who could not find the way out, and ten years later he still cannot.

**Ecology.** A slowly turning cloud of grey stone corridors, as if a tiny maze were folded up and thrown into the air. Each time you look, the walls are slightly different. It is drawn to puzzles, locked doors and dead ends, and tends to settle near them.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry on the highest-Intelligence target who is still free, usually a wizard or an artificer. The best counter is a ball of string held by a friend outside the Maze, because the trapped creature gains Advantage on its escape checks. Killing the living spell brings every victim back at once. A target that makes a single successful check can walk out, so the Maze is a puzzle more than a prison.

***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the living spell fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +11, reach 20 ft. *Hit:* 44 (7d10 + 6) Radiant damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 19, each creature in a 60-foot-radius Sphere centered on a point the living spell can see within 150 feet. *Failure:* 42 (12d6) Radiant damage, and the target has the Blinded condition for 1 minute (repeating the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the condition on a success). \column

**Loot.** *Sun-Seed* (900 gp): a golden orb that slowly heats and glows with sunlight, and keeps a room warm and bright for a month. *Dawn-Glass* (400 gp): a shard of pale yellow glass that casts *daylight* once. It is a favourite with the Order of the Pale Dawn.

**Lore.** On the day of the Catastrophe, a cleric stood on a hill and cast *sunburst* on a swarm of undead. They say the swarm was destroyed, and the sunburst went on.

**Ecology.** A blinding golden sphere with a small, spinning ring at its centre. It shines at night and falls dim in daylight, and no one has gone near it in the bright of noon. It drifts toward undead and seems to hate them.

**Tactics.** It opens by using Spell Mimicry on the biggest group, then uses its Multiattack on whoever has not been blinded. Necrotic damage is its great weakness, since it is Vulnerable to Necrotic. A blindfold removes the blinding effect, though it does impose its own penalties, so a group that can fight blind has the advantage.

**Initiative**  :: +5 (15)

|Int|3|--4|--4|Wis|14|+2|+7|Cha|6|--2|--2|

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Immunities**  :: Psychic; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 120 ft., Passive Perception 12
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 15 (XP 13,000; PB +5)

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the living spell fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

***Spoken Word.*** Spell Mimicry has no effect on a creature that cannot hear the living spell, and the living spell can't use it while it is inside a *silence* spell.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +12, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 34 (6d8 + 7) Psychic damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 6).*** One creature that the living spell can see within 60 feet and that can hear it takes the effect. If the creature has 100 Hit Points or fewer, it dies. Otherwise, it takes 78 (12d12) Psychic damage. A creature that is Deafened, or inside a *silence* spell, can't be targeted.

### Legendary Actions (3/Turn)
{{legend Legendary Action Uses: 3. Immediately after another creature's turn, the living spell can expend a use to take one of the following actions. The living spell regains expended uses at the start of each of its turns.}}

***Magical Strike (Costs 1 Action).*** The living spell makes one Magical Strike attack.

***Dart Away (Costs 1 Action).*** The living spell moves up to half its Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks.

***Whisper (Costs 2 Actions).*** One creature within 60 feet that can hear the living spell makes a DC 20 Wisdom saving throw. *Failure:* 22 (4d10) Psychic damage and the creature has the Frightened condition until the end of its next turn. *Success:* Half damage only.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Last Syllable* (1,200 gp): a tiny crystal that hums with the tone of a spoken word. A caster who holds it gains Advantage on their next Arcana check about necromancy. *Mourner's Quill* (500 gp): a black feather pen that, for a day, writes the name of the dead in silver. A buyer can usually be found in a temple.

**Lore.** It was the last word of a dying wizard who had no more breath for the rest of the spell. It is a single sentence, and nobody who heard it was left alive to tell the next part.

**Ecology.** A tiny, whispering sliver of black light the size of a bird, with a halo of letters in a forgotten script rotating around it. It is fast and nimble, and lives in silent places such as crypts, libraries and the eaves of old towers. It speaks softly, and only once.

**Tactics.** It is Tiny and fast, with high Dexterity and a flying speed of 60 feet. Use *silence*, ear plugs, or the Deafened condition to stop Spell Mimicry; a deaf creature or a creature in a *silence* spell can't be targeted. It targets the creature with the fewest hit points it can find first, so keep wounded allies in cover. Once it has used Spell Mimicry, it flits around and uses Multiattack.

**Immunities**  :: Force; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 120 ft., Passive Perception 12

**CR**  :: 16 (XP 15,000; PB +5)

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the living spell fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

***Unfinished Business.*** A creature within 5 feet of the living spell can use an action to speak the spell's missing final word, making a DC 20 Intelligence (Arcana) check. On a success, the living spell loses Magic Resistance and can't use Spell Mimicry until the end of its next turn.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +12, reach 5 ft. *Hit:* 34 (5d10 + 7) Force damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 6).*** *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 20, each creature within 60 feet of the living spell that is not a Construct or Undead. *Failure:* The target has the Incapacitated condition until the end of its next turn. *Success:* No effect. Immediately after this action, the living spell takes an extra turn. It can't use Spell Mimicry during that turn.

### Legendary Actions (3/Turn)
{{legend Legendary Action Uses: 3. Immediately after another creature's turn, the living spell can expend a use to take one of the following actions. The living spell regains expended uses at the start of each of its turns.}}

***Hand of the Clock (Costs 1 Action).*** The living spell makes one Magical Strike attack.

***Stutter (Costs 1 Action).*** The living spell moves up to half its Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks.

***Skipped Beat (Costs 2 Actions).*** One creature within 60 feet makes a DC 20 Wisdom saving throw. *Failure:* The creature can't take Reactions or Bonus Actions until the end of its next turn.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Stopped Hourglass* (1,500 gp): an hourglass with sand that hangs in the air. Turned over, the sand stops for 1 round, giving its holder Advantage on their next initiative roll if used before combat. *Moment of Silence* (700 gp): a little bell that makes no sound when it rings, and that for 1 minute makes the area around it silent; a bard may treasure it.

**Lore.** Clockmakers swear it was one of the few spells cast in time. It was cast to give a mage time to think, and it has been thinking since.

**Ecology.** A clear bubble of silvery nothing in which the air stands still and dust hangs motionless. It makes no sound and no breeze, and the ticking of nearby clocks stops. It drifts in quiet, deserted places such as clock towers, libraries and long-closed shops.

**Tactics.** It uses Spell Mimicry to freeze everyone it can, then enjoys an extra turn. It is not unbeatable. It is a spell that never finished, and a creature within 5 feet that succeeds on a DC 20 Intelligence (Arcana) check as an action can speak the final word. If it does, the living spell loses Magic Resistance and can't use Spell Mimicry until the end of its next turn.

**Resistances**  :: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Vulnerabilities**  :: Cold
**Immunities**  :: Fire; Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses**  :: Darkvision 120 ft., Passive Perception 12
**Languages**  :: —
**CR**  :: 17 (XP 18,000; PB +6)

### Traits
***Amorphous.*** The living spell can move through a space as narrow as 1 inch without squeezing.

***Magic Resistance.*** The living spell has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the living spell fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The living spell makes two Magical Strike attacks.

***Magical Strike.*** *Melee Attack Roll:* +13, reach 20 ft. *Hit:* 51 (8d10 + 7) Fire damage.

***Spell Mimicry (Recharge 5–6).*** The living spell marks up to four points it can see within 150 feet with glowing sigils. At the end of its next turn, a meteor falls on each marked point, exploding in a 20-foot-radius Sphere. *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 21, each creature in a Sphere (a creature in more than one is affected only once). *Failure:* 35 (10d6) Fire damage plus 35 (10d6) Bludgeoning damage. *Success:* Half damage. Flammable objects in the areas that aren't worn or carried ignite. A creature that uses an action in a sigil's space to make a DC 21 Intelligence (Arcana) check can erase it, cancelling the meteor that would fall there.

### Legendary Actions (3/Turn)
{{legend Legendary Action Uses: 3. Immediately after another creature's turn, the living spell can expend a use to take one of the following actions. The living spell regains expended uses at the start of each of its turns.}}

***Magical Strike (Costs 1 Action).*** The living spell makes one Magical Strike attack.

***Burning Wake (Costs 1 Action).*** The living spell moves up to half its Speed. Each creature within 5 feet of the path takes 10 (3d6) Fire damage (no save).

***Falling Star (Costs 2 Actions).*** A meteor falls on a point the living spell can see within 150 feet, exploding in a 20-foot-radius Sphere. *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 21, each creature in the area. *Failure:* 21 (6d6) Fire damage plus 21 (6d6) Bludgeoning damage. *Success:* Half damage.

}}

\column

**Loot.** *Star-Iron Nugget* (2,000 gp): a dense, glowing lump of meteoric iron. A smith can make it into a weapon that deals an extra 1d6 Fire damage once per day. *Comet-Glass* (800 gp): a smoky piece of glass that, if shattered, creates a 10-foot-radius blaze of light for 1 minute. *Rubble Core* (350 gp): a stone fragment from the heart of a meteor, which can serve as a component for *meteor swarm* or *earthquake*.

**Lore.** On the day of the Catastrophe a mage looked up and called down the sky on an army. The sky answered, and then did not stop answering. Villagers who have seen it say that the stars fall down one by one, as if each one is being lowered from a thread.

**Ecology.** A massive swirl of burning rock and white-gold fire, with four rough fragments orbiting a glowing core. It is as big as a house. It moves slowly and heavily across open plains, mountains and places where the ground has been burned flat. Its path is traced by craters.

**Tactics.** It is telegraphed: first it marks four points with sigils and the meteors land at the end of its next turn, which gives the party a round to run, erase a sigil, or kill it. It uses Multiattack in the meantime. Cold damage hurts it, because it is Vulnerable to Cold, and a party that deals 100 damage in a round breaks the sequence entirely. Keep moving and do not stand together.
